Question 11 | Bank ID: Y03-011 | Task: 3.1
You’re worried that updates to the important data you store in S3 might incorrectly over- write existing files. What must you do to protect objects in S3 buckets from being acciden- tally lost?
A. Nothing. S3 protects existing files by default.
B. Nothing. S3 saves older versions of your files by default.
C. Enable versioning.
D. Enable file overwrite protection.

Book answer: C
Practice answer: C
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: Enable versioning on the bucket to retain recoverable object versions after overwrites or deletes. It is not enabled separately on individual objects; lifecycle rules can still expire versions.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Incorrect for this question. Default strong consistency does not preserve overwritten versions.
B: Incorrect for this question. Versioning must be enabled; it is not on by default.
C: Correct. Enable versioning on the bucket to retain recoverable object versions after overwrites or deletes. It is not enabled separately on individual objects; lifecycle rules can still expire versions.
D: Incorrect for this question. There is no bucket setting called file overwrite protection; Object Lock has a different purpose.

AWS documentation: S3 — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonS3/latest/userguide/Welcome.html

Question 14 | Bank ID: Y03-014 | Task: 3.1
Which of the following are the recommended methods for providing secure and controlled access to your buckets? (Choose two.)
A. S3 access control lists (ACLs)
B. S3 bucket policies
C. IAM policies
D. Security groups
E. AWS Key Management Service

Book answer: B, C
Practice answer: B, C
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: ACLs are a legacy feature that isn’t as flexible as IAM or S3 bucket polices. Security groups are not used with S3 buckets. KMS is an encryption key management tool and isn’t used for authentication.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Incorrect for this question. ACLs are legacy; bucket-owner-enforced Object Ownership disables them by default.
B: Correct. ACLs are a legacy feature that isn’t as flexible as IAM or S3 bucket polices. Security groups are not used with S3 buckets. KMS is an encryption key management tool and isn’t used for authentication.
C: Correct. ACLs are a legacy feature that isn’t as flexible as IAM or S3 bucket polices. Security groups are not used with S3 buckets. KMS is an encryption key management tool and isn’t used for authentication.
D: Incorrect for this question. Security groups do not attach directly to S3 buckets.
E: Incorrect for this question. KMS controls key use, not the complete S3 object authorization policy.

References:
AWS documentation: S3 — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonS3/latest/userguide/Welcome.html

Question 16 | Bank ID: Y03-016 | Task: 3.1
You don’t want to open up the contents of an S3 bucket to anyone on the Internet, but you need to share the data with specific clients. Generating and then sending them a presigned URL is a perfect solution. Assuming you didn’t explicitly set a value, how long will the presigned URL remain valid?
A. 24 hours
B. 3,600 seconds
C. 5 minutes
D. 360 seconds

Book answer: B
Practice answer: B
Review: Corrected | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: The AWS CLI default is 3600 seconds. Other signing tools can use different defaults; temporary credential expiry can shorten effective validity. Practice stem clarified; original retained below.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Incorrect for this question. Defaults depend on the signing tool; 24 hours is not universal.
B: Correct. 3600 seconds is the AWS CLI default, not every tool's default. The AWS CLI default is 3600 seconds. Other signing tools can use different defaults; temporary credential expiry can shorten effective validity.
C: Incorrect for this question. Five minutes is not the CLI default.
D: Incorrect for this question. 360 seconds is not the CLI default.

Practice wording:

A. 24 hours
B. 3,600 seconds
C. 5 minutes
D. 360 seconds

References:
AWS reference for this review — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/cli/latest/reference/s3/presign.html

Question 17 | Bank ID: Y03-017 | Task: 3.1

Practice answer: A, D

Rationale: Route 53 supplies DNS names and ACM supplies TLS certificates through a supported front-end such as CloudFront. An S3 website endpoint itself does not support attaching an ACM certificate.

C: Incorrect for this question. RDS is unnecessary for static content delivery.
D: Correct. Route 53 supplies DNS names and ACM supplies TLS certificates through a supported front-end such as CloudFront. An S3 website endpoint itself does not support attaching an ACM certificate.
E: Incorrect for this question. KMS protects stored keys/data rather than providing public website TLS and DNS.

Question 1 | Bank ID: Y04-001 | Task: 3.4
What is the range of allowed IPv4 prefix lengths for a VPC CIDR block?
A. /16 to /28
B. /16 to /56
C. /8 to /30
D. /56 only

Book answer: A
Practice answer: A
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: The allowed range of prefix lengths for a VPC CIDR is between /16 and /28 inclusive. The maximum possible prefix length for an IP subnet is /32, so /56 is not a valid length.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Correct. The allowed range of prefix lengths for a VPC CIDR is between /16 and /28 inclusive. The maximum possible prefix length for an IP subnet is /32, so /56 is not a valid length.
B: Incorrect for this question. /56 is associated with VPC IPv6 allocation, not the IPv4 subnet range.
C: Incorrect for this question. /8 and /30 fall outside VPC IPv4 CIDR limits.
D: Incorrect for this question. /56 is not an IPv4 prefix length.

AWS documentation: VPC — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/vpc/latest/userguide/what-is-amazon-vpc.html

CHAPTER 4: Amazon Virtual Private Cloud (VPC)
Question 2 | Bank ID: Y04-002 | Task: 3.4
You’ve created a VPC with the CIDR 192.168.16.0/24. You want to assign a secondary CIDR to this VPC. Which CIDR can you use?
A. 172.31.0.0/16
B. 192.168.0.0/16
C. 192.168.0.0/24
D. 192.168.16.0/23

Book answer: C
Practice answer: C
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: A secondary CIDR may come from the same RFC 1918 address range as the primary, but it may not overlap with the primary CIDR. 192.168.0.0/24 comes from the same address range (192.168.0.0–192.168.255.255) as the primary and does not overlap with 192.168.16.0/24; 192.168.0.0/16 and 192.168.16.0/23 both overlap with 192.168.16.0/24; and 172.31.0.0/16 is not in the same range as the primary CIDR.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Incorrect for this question. VPC secondary RFC 1918 range-association restrictions exclude this different private family here.
B: Incorrect for this question. This range overlaps the existing primary range.
C: Correct. A secondary CIDR may come from the same RFC 1918 address range as the primary, but it may not overlap with the primary CIDR. 192.168.0.0/24 comes from the same address range (192.168.0.0–192.168.255.255) as the primary and does not overlap with 192.168.16.0/24; 192.168.0.0/16 and 192.168.16.0/23 both overlap with 192.168.16.0/24; and 172.31.0.0/16 is not in the same range as the primary CIDR.
D: Incorrect for this question. This supernet overlaps the existing primary range.

References:
AWS documentation: VPC — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/vpc/latest/userguide/what-is-amazon-vpc.html

You need to create two subnets in a VPC that has a CIDR of 10.0.0.0/16. Which of the fol- lowing CIDRs can you assign to one of the subnets while leaving room for an additional subnet? (Choose all that apply.)
A. 10.0.0.0/24

C. 10.0.0.0/16

Book answer: A, D
Practice answer: A, D

Rationale: Options A and D (10.0.0.0/24 and 10.0.0.0/23) are within the VPC CIDR and leave room for a second subnet; 10.0.0.0/8 is wrong because prefix lengths less than /16 aren’t allowed; and 10.0.0.0/16 doesn’t leave room for another subnet.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Correct. Options A and D (10.0.0.0/24 and 10.0.0.0/23) are within the VPC CIDR and leave room for a second subnet; 10.0.0.0/8 is wrong because prefix lengths less than /16 aren’t allowed; and 10.0.0.0/16 doesn’t leave room for another subnet.
B: Incorrect for this question. A /8 extends beyond the VPC's /16 address block.
C: Incorrect for this question. This consumes the entire VPC range and leaves no second subnet.
D: Correct. Options A and D (10.0.0.0/24 and 10.0.0.0/23) are within the VPC CIDR and leave room for a second subnet; 10.0.0.0/8 is wrong because prefix lengths less than /16 aren’t allowed; and 10.0.0.0/16 doesn’t leave room for another subnet.

What is the relationship between a subnet and an availability zone?

Question 5 | Bank ID: Y04-005 | Task: 3.4
Which is true regarding an elastic network interface?
A. It must have a private IP address from the subnet that it resides in.
B. It cannot exist independently of an instance.
C. It can be connected to multiple subnets.
D. It can have multiple IP addresses from different subnets.

Book answer: A
Practice answer: A
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: Every ENI must have a primary private IP address. It can have secondary IP addresses, but all addresses must come from the subnet the ENI resides in. Once created, the ENI cannot be moved to a different subnet. An ENI can be created independently of an instance and later attached to an instance.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Correct. Every ENI must have a primary private IP address. It can have secondary IP addresses, but all addresses must come from the subnet the ENI resides in. Once created, the ENI cannot be moved to a different subnet. An ENI can be created independently of an instance and later attached to an instance.
B: Incorrect for this question. An ENI can exist unattached.
C: Incorrect for this question. One ENI belongs to one subnet.
D: Incorrect for this question. An ENI's assigned private addresses come from its own subnet.

Question 6 | Bank ID: Y04-006 | Task: 1.2
Which of the following statements is true of security groups?
A. Only one security group can be attached to an ENI.
B. A security group must always be attached to an ENI.
C. A security group can be attached to a subnet.
D. Every VPC contains a default security group.

Book answer: D
Practice answer: D
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: Each VPC contains a default security group that can’t be deleted. You can create a secu- rity group by itself without attaching it to anything. But if you want to use it, you must attach it to an ENI. You also attach multiple security groups to the same ENI.

Option-by-option reasons:

C: Incorrect for this question. NACLs attach to subnets; security groups attach to interfaces/resources.
D: Correct. Each VPC contains a default security group that can’t be deleted. You can create a secu- rity group by itself without attaching it to anything. But if you want to use it, you must attach it to an ENI. You also attach multiple security groups to the same ENI.

CHAPTER 4: Amazon Virtual Private Cloud (VPC)
Question 7 | Bank ID: Y04-007 | Task: 1.2
How does an NACL differ from a security group?
A. An NACL is stateless.
B. An NACL is stateful.
C. An NACL is attached to an ENI.
D. An NACL can be associated with only one subnet.

Book answer: A
Practice answer: A
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: An NACL is stateless, meaning it doesn’t track a connection state. Every inbound rule must have a corresponding outbound rule to permit traffic, and vice versa. An NACL is attached to a subnet, whereas a security group is attached to an ENI. An NACL can be asso- ciated with multiple subnets, but a subnet can have only one NACL.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Correct. An NACL is stateless, meaning it doesn’t track a connection state. Every inbound rule must have a corresponding outbound rule to permit traffic, and vice versa. An NACL is attached to a subnet, whereas a security group is attached to an ENI. An NACL can be asso- ciated with multiple subnets, but a subnet can have only one NACL.
B: Incorrect for this question. Security groups are stateful; NACLs require return-path rules.
C: Incorrect for this question. NACLs associate with subnets rather than individual ENIs.
D: Incorrect for this question. One NACL can associate with multiple subnets.

References:
AWS documentation: VPC — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/vpc/latest/userguide/what-is-amazon-vpc.html

Question 8 | Bank ID: Y04-008 | Task: 3.4
What is an Internet gateway?
A. A resource that grants instances in multiple VPCs’ Internet access
B. An implied router
C. A physical router
D. A VPC resource with no management IP address

Book answer: D
Practice answer: D
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: An Internet gateway has no management IP address. It can be associated with only one VPC at a time and so cannot grant Internet access to instances in multiple VPCs. It is a logical VPC resource and not a virtual or physical router.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Incorrect for this question. An Internet gateway attaches to one VPC at a time.
B: Incorrect for this question. The VPC router is a different logical component.
C: Incorrect for this question. It is a managed logical resource rather than a customer-controlled physical router.
D: Correct. An Internet gateway has no management IP address. It can be associated with only one VPC at a time and so cannot grant Internet access to instances in multiple VPCs. It is a logical VPC resource and not a virtual or physical router.

Question 9 | Bank ID: Y04-009 | Task: 3.4
What is the destination for a default IPv4 route?
A. 0.0.0.0/0
B. ::0/0
C. An Internet gateway
D. The IP address of the implied router

Book answer: A
Practice answer: A
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: The destination 0.0.0.0/0 matches all IP prefixes and hence covers all publicly accessible hosts on the Internet. ::0/0 is an IPv6 prefix, not an IPv4 prefix. An Internet gateway is the target of the default route, not the destination.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Correct. The destination 0.0.0.0/0 matches all IP prefixes and hence covers all publicly accessible hosts on the Internet. ::0/0 is an IPv6 prefix, not an IPv4 prefix. An Internet gateway is the target of the default route, not the destination.
B: Incorrect for this question. This denotes an IPv6 default destination.
C: Incorrect for this question. The gateway is a target rather than the destination CIDR.
D: Incorrect for this question. The router address is not the all-destinations IPv4 prefix.

References:
AWS documentation: VPC — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/vpc/latest/userguide/what-is-amazon-vpc.html

How can you assign a public IP address to a running instance that doesn’t have one?

CHAPTER 4: Amazon Virtual Private Cloud (VPC)
Question 13 | Bank ID: Y04-013 | Task: 3.4
When an instance with an automatically assigned public IP sends a packet to another instance’s EIP, what source address does the destination instance see?
A. The public IP
B. The EIP
C. The private IP
D. 0.0.0.0

Book answer: A
Practice answer: A
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: Internet- bound traffic from an instance with an automatically assigned public IP will traverse an Internet gateway that will perform NAT. The source address will be the instance’s public IP. An instance with an automatically assigned public IP cannot also have an EIP. The NAT process will replace the private IP source address with the public IP. Option D, 0.0.0.0, is not a valid source address.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Correct. Internet- bound traffic from an instance with an automatically assigned public IP will traverse an Internet gateway that will perform NAT. The source address will be the instance’s public IP. An instance with an automatically assigned public IP cannot also have an EIP. The NAT process will replace the private IP source address with the public IP. Option D, 0.0.0.0, is not a valid source address.
B: Incorrect for this question. The source has an automatic public IP, not its own EIP.
C: Incorrect for this question. Traffic addressed through a public EIP is translated to use the public source.
D: Incorrect for this question. 0.0.0.0 is not a normal routed packet's usable source here.

References:
AWS documentation: VPC — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/vpc/latest/userguide/what-is-amazon-vpc.html

Question 14 | Bank ID: Y04-014 | Task: 3.4
Why must a NAT gateway reside in a different subnet than an instance that uses it?
A. Both must use different default gateways.
B. Both must use different NACLs.
C. Both must use different security groups.
D. The NAT gateway requires a public interface and a private interface.

Book answer: A
Practice answer: A
Review: Corrected | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: Private-subnet default routes point to the NAT gateway; the NAT gateway subnet routes to the Internet gateway. Using the same route table would create an invalid or looping egress design. Regional NAT gateways use a different model. Practice stem clarified; original retained below.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Correct. Different route tables prevent routing the NAT gateway back through itself. Private-subnet default routes point to the NAT gateway; the NAT gateway subnet routes to the Internet gateway. Using the same route table would create an invalid or looping egress design. Regional NAT gateways use a different model.
B: Incorrect for this question. Different NACLs are not required.
C: Incorrect for this question. Security groups do not attach to NAT gateways.
D: Incorrect for this question. A NAT gateway does not require two customer-created ENIs.

Practice wording:
Why should a traditional zonal public NAT gateway be in a public subnet separate from private instances using it for Internet egress?
A. Both must use different default gateways.
B. Both must use different NACLs.
C. Both must use different security groups.
D. The NAT gateway requires a public interface and a private interface.

Question 16 | Bank ID: Y04-016 | Task: 3.4
Which VPC resource performs network address translation?
A. Internet gateway
B. Route table
C. EIP
D. ENI

Book answer: A
Practice answer: A
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: An Internet gateway performs NAT for instances that have a public IP address. A route table defines how traffic from instances is forwarded. An EIP is a public IP address and can’t perform NAT. An ENI is a network interface and doesn’t perform NAT.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Correct. An Internet gateway performs NAT for instances that have a public IP address. A route table defines how traffic from instances is forwarded. An EIP is a public IP address and can’t perform NAT. An ENI is a network interface and doesn’t perform NAT.
B: Incorrect for this question. A route table chooses targets rather than translating packets.
C: Incorrect for this question. An EIP is an address allocation rather than the translation engine.
D: Incorrect for this question. An ENI supplies network attachment rather than Internet NAT itself.

References:
AWS documentation: VPC — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/vpc/latest/userguide/what-is-amazon-vpc.html

CHAPTER 4: Amazon Virtual Private Cloud (VPC)
Question 17 | Bank ID: Y04-017 | Task: 3.4
What must you do to configure a NAT instance after creating it?
A. Disable the source/destination check on its ENI.
B. Enable the source/destination check on its ENI.
C. Create a default route in its route table with a NAT gateway as the target.
D. Assign a primary private IP address to the instance.

Book answer: A
Practice answer: A
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: The source/destination check on the NAT instance’s ENI must be disabled to allow the in- stance to receive traffic not destined for its IP and to send traffic using a source address that it doesn’t own. The NAT instance’s default route must point to an Internet gateway as the target. You can’t assign a primary private IP address after the instance is created.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Correct. The source/destination check on the NAT instance’s ENI must be disabled to allow the in- stance to receive traffic not destined for its IP and to send traffic using a source address that it doesn’t own. The NAT instance’s default route must point to an Internet gateway as the target. You can’t assign a primary private IP address after the instance is created.
B: Incorrect for this question. Forwarding translated traffic requires disabling this check.
C: Incorrect for this question. A public NAT instance normally routes outbound traffic to an Internet gateway.
D: Incorrect for this question. The primary private IP is assigned at launch already.

References:

CHAPTER 4: Amazon Virtual Private Cloud (VPC)
Question 18 | Bank ID: Y04-018 | Task: 3.4
Which of the following is true regarding VPC peering?
A. Transitive routing is not supported.
B. A VPC peering connection requires a public IP address.
C. You can peer up to three VPCs using a single peering connection.
D. You can use a peering connection to share an Internet gateway among multiple VPCs.

Book answer: A
Practice answer: A
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: VPC peering joins two VPCs directly and does not support transitive routing or sharing a peer Internet gateway. Appropriate routes and security rules can support private connectivity between permitted resources.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Correct. VPC peering joins two VPCs directly and does not support transitive routing or sharing a peer Internet gateway. Appropriate routes and security rules can support private connectivity between permitted resources.
B: Incorrect for this question. Peering communicates using private addresses.
C: Incorrect for this question. One peering connection joins exactly two VPCs.
D: Incorrect for this question. Peering cannot provide edge-to-edge sharing of an Internet gateway.

AWS documentation: VPC — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/vpc/latest/userguide/what-is-amazon-vpc.html

CHAPTER 4: Amazon Virtual Private Cloud (VPC)
Question 19 | Bank ID: Y04-019 | Task: 3.4
You’ve created one VPC peering connection between two VPCs. What must you do to use this connection for bidirectional instance- to- instance communication? (Choose all that apply.)
A. Create two routes with the peering connection as the target.
B. Create only one default route with the peering connection as the target.
C. Create another peering connection between the VPCs.
D. Configure the instances’ security groups correctly.

Book answer: A, D
Practice answer: A, D
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: Each peered VPC needs a route to the CIDR of its peer; therefore, you must create two routes with the peering connection as the target. Creating only one route is not sufficient to enable bidirectional communication. Additionally, the instances’ security groups must allow for bidirectional communication. You can’t create more than one peering connection between a pair of VPCs.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Correct. Each peered VPC needs a route to the CIDR of its peer; therefore, you must create two routes with the peering connection as the target. Creating only one route is not sufficient to enable bidirectional communication. Additionally, the instances’ security groups must allow for bidirectional communication. You can’t create more than one peering connection between a pair of VPCs.
B: Incorrect for this question. Both sides need appropriate peer-CIDR routes, not one unilateral default route.
C: Incorrect for this question. A single accepted peering connection is bidirectional.
D: Correct. Each peered VPC needs a route to the CIDR of its peer; therefore, you must create two routes with the peering connection as the target. Creating only one route is not sufficient to enable bidirectional communication. Additionally, the instances’ security groups must allow for bidirectional communication. You can’t create more than one peering connection between a pair of VPCs.

References:
AWS documentation: VPC — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/vpc/latest/userguide/what-is-amazon-vpc.html

CHAPTER 5: Database Services
Question 1 | Bank ID: Y05-001 | Task: 3.3
In a relational database, a row may also be called what? (Choose two.)
A. Record
B. Attribute
C. Tuple
D. Table

Book answer: A, C
Practice answer: A, C
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: Different relational databases use different terminology. A row, record, and tuple all describe an ordered set of columns. An attribute is another term for column. A table contains rows and columns.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Correct. Different relational databases use different terminology. A row, record, and tuple all describe an ordered set of columns. An attribute is another term for column. A table contains rows and columns.
B: Incorrect for this question. An attribute is a column rather than a row.
C: Correct. Different relational databases use different terminology. A row, record, and tuple all describe an ordered set of columns. An attribute is another term for column. A table contains rows and columns.
D: Incorrect for this question. A table is a collection of rows and columns.

References:
AWS documentation: RDS — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/Welcome.html

Question 2 | Bank ID: Y05-002 | Task: 3.3
What must every relational database table contain?
A. A foreign key
B. A primary key
C. An attribute
D. A row

Book answer: C
Practice answer: C
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: A table must contain at least one attribute or column. Primary and foreign keys are used for relating data in different tables, but they’re not required. A row can exist within a table, but a table doesn’t need a row in order to exist.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Incorrect for this question. A table need not reference another table.
B: Incorrect for this question. SQL permits tables without a declared primary key.
C: Correct. A table must contain at least one attribute or column. Primary and foreign keys are used for relating data in different tables, but they’re not required. A row can exist within a table, but a table doesn’t need a row in order to exist.
D: Incorrect for this question. An empty table can have no rows.

Question 4 | Bank ID: Y05-004 | Task: 3.3
Which relational database type is optimized to handle multiple transactions per second?
A. Offline transaction processing (OLTP)
B. Online transaction processing (OLTP)
C. Online analytic processing (OLAP)
D. Key/value store

Book answer: B
Practice answer: B
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: Online transaction processing databases are designed to handle multiple transactions per second. Online analytics processing databases are for complex queries against large data sets. A key/value store such as DynamoDB can handle multiple transactions per second, but it’s not a relational database. There’s no such thing as an offline transaction processing database.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Incorrect for this question. OLTP means online, not offline, transaction processing.
B: Correct. Online transaction processing databases are designed to handle multiple transactions per second. Online analytics processing databases are for complex queries against large data sets. A key/value store such as DynamoDB can handle multiple transactions per second, but it’s not a relational database. There’s no such thing as an offline transaction processing database.
C: Incorrect for this question. OLAP favors analytical scans and aggregation.
D: Incorrect for this question. A key/value store is not the relational workload category requested.

Question 7 | Bank ID: Y05-007 | Task: 3.3
Which storage engine should you use with MySQL, Aurora, and MariaDB for maximum compatibility with RDS?

B. XtraDB
C. InnoDB
D. PostgreSQL

Book answer: C
Practice answer: C
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: InnoDB is the only storage engine Amazon recommends for MySQL and MariaDB deployments in RDS and the only engine Aurora supports. MyISAM is another storage engine that works with MySQL but is not compatible with automated backups. XtraDB is another storage engine for MariaDB, but Amazon no longer recommends it. The PostgreSQL database engine uses its own storage engine by the same name and is not compatible with other database engines.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Incorrect for this question. MyISAM lacks the transaction/recovery characteristics required by Aurora.
B: Incorrect for this question. XtraDB is not the common supported engine across these services.
C: Correct. InnoDB is the only storage engine Amazon recommends for MySQL and MariaDB deployments in RDS and the only engine Aurora supports. MyISAM is another storage engine that works with MySQL but is not compatible with automated backups. XtraDB is another storage engine for MariaDB, but Amazon no longer recommends it. The PostgreSQL database engine uses its own storage engine by the same name and is not compatible with other database engines.
D: Incorrect for this question. PostgreSQL is a database engine, not a MySQL storage engine.

References:
AWS documentation: RDS — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/Welcome.html

Question 10 | Bank ID: Y05-010 | Task: 3.3
If a MariaDB database running in RDS needs to write 200 MB of data every second, how many IOPS should you provision using io1 storage to sustain this performance?
A. 12,800
B. 25,600
C. 200
D. 16

Book answer: A
Practice answer: A
Review: Corrected | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: 200 times 1024 KiB/s divided by 16 KiB per operation equals 12,800 IOPS. Real sizing must also consider instance and storage throughput limits. Practice stem clarified; original retained below.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Correct. At an assumed 16 KiB operation size, 200 MiB/s divided by 16 KiB is 12,800 operations/s. 200 times 1024 KiB/s divided by 16 KiB per operation equals 12,800 IOPS. Real sizing must also consider instance and storage throughput limits.
B: Incorrect for this question. 25,600 assumes 8 KiB operations, which the question does not specify.
C: Incorrect for this question. 200 confuses megabytes per second with operations per second.
D: Incorrect for this question. 16 confuses an assumed operation size with the IOPS count.

Practice wording:
Assuming each database write is 16 KiB, how many write IOPS are needed for 200 MiB/s, before accounting for other limits?
A. 12,800
B. 25,600
C. 200
D. 16

References:

Practice answer: A

In a multi- AZ deployment using Oracle, how is data replicated?
A. Synchronously from the primary instance to a read replica
B. Synchronously using a cluster volume

D: Correct. Multi- AZ deployments using Oracle, PostgreSQL, MariaDB, MySQL, or Microsoft SQL Server replicate data synchronously from the primary to a standby instance. Only a multi- AZ deployment using Aurora uses a cluster volume and replicates data to a specific type of read replica called an Aurora replica.

Question 15 | Bank ID: Y05-015 | Task: 3.3
Which of the following occurs when you restore a failed database instance from a snapshot?
A. RDS restores the snapshot to a new instance.
B. RDS restores the snapshot to the failed instance.
C. RDS restores only the individual databases to a new instance.
D. RDS deletes the snapshot.

Book answer: A
Practice answer: A
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: When you restore from a snapshot, RDS creates a new instance and doesn’t make any changes to the failed instance. A snapshot is a copy of the entire instance, not just a copy of the individual databases. RDS does not delete a snapshot after restoring from it.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Correct. When you restore from a snapshot, RDS creates a new instance and doesn’t make any changes to the failed instance. A snapshot is a copy of the entire instance, not just a copy of the individual databases. RDS does not delete a snapshot after restoring from it.
B: Incorrect for this question. Snapshot restoration creates a different DB instance.
C: Incorrect for this question. The snapshot restores the instance's data rather than selected individual databases.
D: Incorrect for this question. Restoring does not delete the retained snapshot.

References:
AWS documentation: RDS — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/Welcome.html
AWS documentation: DynamoDB — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/amazondynamodb/latest/developerguide/Introduction.html

CHAPTER 5: Database Services
Question 16 | Bank ID: Y05-016 | Task: 3.3
Which Redshift distribution style stores all tables on all compute nodes?
A. EVEN
B. ALL
C. KEY
D. ODD

Book answer: B
Practice answer: B
Review: Corrected | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: ALL places a full copy of that table on each compute node, trading additional storage and load work for reduced redistribution on appropriate joins. Practice stem clarified; original retained below.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Incorrect for this question. EVEN distributes rows round-robin rather than copies each table.
B: Correct. ALL copies an individual table to each node; it is selected per table. ALL places a full copy of that table on each compute node, trading additional storage and load work for reduced redistribution on appropriate joins.
C: Incorrect for this question. KEY distributes rows according to a distribution column.
D: Incorrect for this question. ODD is not a Redshift distribution style.

Practice wording:
Which Redshift distribution style copies the entire selected table to each compute node?
A. EVEN
B. ALL
C. KEY
D. ODD

References:
AWS documentation: Redshift — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/redshift/latest/mgmt/welcome.html

Question 18 | Bank ID: Y05-018 | Task: 3.3
Which is true regarding a primary key in a nonrelational database? (Choose all that apply.)
A. It’s required to uniquely identify an item.
B. It must be unique within the table.
C. It’s used to correlate data across different tables.
D. Its data type can vary within a table.

Book answer: A, B
Practice answer: A, B
Review: Corrected | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: The complete key uniquely identifies an item within its table. A composite key comprises partition and sort keys; partition values alone can repeat. Practice stem clarified; original retained below.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Correct. For DynamoDB, the full primary key identifies an item uniquely. The complete key uniquely identifies an item within its table. A composite key comprises partition and sort keys; partition values alone can repeat.
B: Correct. For DynamoDB, the full primary key is unique; a partition key alone may repeat. The complete key uniquely identifies an item within its table. A composite key comprises partition and sort keys; partition values alone can repeat.
C: Incorrect for this question. Cross-table relationships are not the main DynamoDB primary-key function.
D: Incorrect for this question. A DynamoDB key attribute has a fixed scalar type within an index/table.

Question 19 | Bank ID: Y05-019 | Task: 3.3
In a DynamoDB table containing orders, which key would be most appropriate for storing an order date?
A. Partition key
B. Sort key
C. Hash key
D. Simple primary key

Book answer: B
Practice answer: B
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: A date-based sort key can support chronological queries within an appropriate partition, often a customer/order group. Include sufficient uniqueness for multiple orders with the same timestamp; a partition key itself need not be globally unique in a composite-key table.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Incorrect for this question. A customer or account partition key often groups related orders.
B: Correct. A date-based sort key can support chronological queries within an appropriate partition, often a customer/order group. Include sufficient uniqueness for multiple orders with the same timestamp; a partition key itself need not be globally unique in a composite-key table.
C: Incorrect for this question. Hash key is another name for partition key, not the ordering component.
D: Incorrect for this question. A simple date-only key would limit uniqueness and query design.

Question 20 | Bank ID: Y05-020 | Task: 3.3
When creating a DynamoDB table, how many read capacity units should you provision to be able to sustain strongly consistent reads of 11 KB per second?
A. 3
B. 2
C. 1
D. 0

Book answer: A
Practice answer: A
Review: Corrected | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: Strongly consistent reads consume one RCU per 4 KiB, rounded up for each item. An 11 KiB item requires ceiling(11/4), or three RCUs. Practice stem clarified; original retained below.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Correct. Three RCUs cover a single strongly consistent read of an 11 KiB item per second. Strongly consistent reads consume one RCU per 4 KiB, rounded up for each item. An 11 KiB item requires ceiling(11/4), or three RCUs.
B: Incorrect for this question. Two RCUs cover only 8 KiB for one strongly consistent item read.
C: Incorrect for this question. One RCU covers only 4 KiB for one strongly consistent item read.
D: Incorrect for this question. Zero provisioned RCUs does not support this provisioned-mode workload.

Practice wording:
How many provisioned read capacity units sustain one strongly consistent read of an 11 KiB DynamoDB item per second?
A. 3
B. 2
C. 1
D. 0

Which DynamoDB index type allows the partition and hash key to differ from the base table?

D. Global secondary index

Book answer: D
Practice answer: D

D: Correct. A GSI can use different partition and sort keys; hash and partition are synonyms. A global secondary index defines an alternative key schema. A local secondary index retains the table partition key and changes the sort key.

Question 23 | Bank ID: Y05-023 | Task: 3.3
To ensure the best performance, in which of the following situations would you choose to store data in a NoSQL database instead of a relational database?
A. You need to perform a variety of complex queries against the data.
B. You need to query data based on only one attribute.
C. You need to store JSON documents.

Book answer: B

C: Study note. Both SQL engines and NoSQL stores can support JSON; this alone is insufficient.
D: Study note. Multiple applications can use either database category.

CHAPTER 5: Database Services
Question 24 | Bank ID: Y05-024 | Task: 3.3
What type of database can discover how different items are related to each other?
A. SQL
B. Relational
C. Document- oriented store
D. Graph

Book answer: D
Practice answer: D
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: A graph database is a type of nonrelational database that discovers relationships among items. A document- oriented store is a nonrelational database that analyzes and extracts data from documents. Relational databases can enforce relationships between records but don’t discover them. A SQL database is a type of relational database.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Incorrect for this question. SQL is a query language, not a relationship-specialized database category.
B: Incorrect for this question. Relational databases express joins, but graph engines specialize in relationship traversal.
C: Incorrect for this question. Document stores organize documents rather than explicit graph edges.
D: Correct. A graph database is a type of nonrelational database that discovers relationships among items. A document- oriented store is a nonrelational database that analyzes and extracts data from documents. Relational databases can enforce relationships between records but don’t discover them. A SQL database is a type of relational database.

Question 1 | Bank ID: Y06-001 | Task: 1.1
Which of the following is the greatest risk posed by using your AWS account root user for day- to- day operations?
A. There would be no easy way to control resource usage by project or class.
B. There would be no effective limits on the effect of an action, making it more likely for unintended and unwanted consequences to result.
C. Since root has full permissions over your account resources, an account compromise at the hands of hackers would be catastrophic.
D. It would make it difficult to track which account user is responsible for specific actions.

Book answer: C
Practice answer: No unique defensible key assigned
Review: Review only | Scored practice: No
Rationale: Routine root use increases excessive-privilege, compromise and attribution risks. Several options describe real risks; greatest depends on the threat model. Use federated/role-based least privilege and restrict root to tasks requiring it.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Study note. Projects can still be organized; this misses the unrestricted credential risk.
B: Study note. Unrestricted actions are also a real risk, so greatest risk is subjective.
C: Study note. Full-account compromise is a severe risk from routine root use.
D: Study note. Shared root access also harms attribution, so this is not categorically wrong.

References:
AWS documentation: IAM — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/IAM/latest/UserGuide/access_policies.html

CHAPTER 6: Authentication and Authorization — IAM
Question 2 | Bank ID: Y06-002 | Task: 1.1
You’re trying to create a custom IAM policy to more closely manage access to components in your application stack. Which of the following syntax- related statements is a correct descrip- tion of IAM policies?
A. The Action element refers to the way IAM will react to a request.
B. The * character applies an element globally—a s broadly as possible.
C. The Resource element refers to the third- party identities that will be allowed to access the account.
D. The Effect element refers to the anticipated resource state after a request is granted.

Book answer: B
Practice answer: B
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: The * character does, indeed, represent global application. The Action element refers to the kind of action requested (list, create, etc.), the Resource element refers to the particular AWS account resource that’s the target of the policy, and the Effect element refers to the way IAM should react to a request.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Incorrect for this question. Action identifies API operations, not IAM's decision response.
B: Correct. The * character does, indeed, represent global application. The Action element refers to the kind of action requested (list, create, etc.), the Resource element refers to the particular AWS account resource that’s the target of the policy, and the Effect element refers to the way IAM should react to a request.
C: Incorrect for this question. Resource identifies affected AWS resources rather than trusted external identities.
D: Incorrect for this question. Effect is Allow or Deny, not a resulting resource state.

References:
AWS documentation: IAM — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/IAM/latest/UserGuide/access_policies.html

Question 3 | Bank ID: Y06-003 | Task: 1.1
Which of the following will—w hen executed on its own— prevent an IAM user with no exist- ing policies from launching an EC2 instance? (Choose three.)
A. Attach no policies to the user.
B. Attach two policies to the user, with one policy permitting full EC2 access and the other permitting IAM password changes but denying EC2 access.
C. Attach a single policy permitting the user to create S3 buckets.
D. Attach the AdministratorAccess policy.
E. Associate an IAM action statement blocking all EC2 access to the user’s account.

Book answer: A, B, C
Practice answer: A, B, C
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: With no other permission sources, an action without an Allow is implicitly denied. An explicit Deny overrides Allow, and an S3-only policy does not allow EC2 launches. Policy statements do contain Action elements; the last option is malformed rather than a real standalone deny mechanism.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Correct. With no other permission sources, an action without an Allow is implicitly denied. An explicit Deny overrides Allow, and an S3-only policy does not allow EC2 launches. Policy statements do contain Action elements; the last option is malformed rather than a real standalone deny mechanism.
B: Correct. With no other permission sources, an action without an Allow is implicitly denied. An explicit Deny overrides Allow, and an S3-only policy does not allow EC2 launches. Policy statements do contain Action elements; the last option is malformed rather than a real standalone deny mechanism.
C: Correct. With no other permission sources, an action without an Allow is implicitly denied. An explicit Deny overrides Allow, and an S3-only policy does not allow EC2 launches. Policy statements do contain Action elements; the last option is malformed rather than a real standalone deny mechanism.
D: Incorrect for this question. AdministratorAccess grants EC2 actions rather than blocks them.
E: Incorrect for this question. This is malformed as a standalone IAM mechanism; an explicit valid Deny statement would also block access.

References:
AWS documentation: EC2 — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AWSEC2/latest/UserGuide/concepts.html
AWS documentation: IAM — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/IAM/latest/UserGuide/access_policies.html

D: Correct. Revoke the former employee identity, secure root credentials/MFA and replace credentials that may have been exposed. Delete unnecessary root access keys. Policies need not all be recreated; other users credentials require action if exposure is suspected.
E: Correct. Rotate exposed keys, especially root keys; remove unnecessary root keys rather than keep them. Question 12 | Bank ID: Y06-012 | Task: 1.1
You need to create a custom IAM policy to give one of your developers limited access to your DynamoDB resources. Which of the following elements will not play any role in crafting an IAM policy?
A. Action
B. Region
C. Effect
D. Resource

Book answer: B
Practice answer: No unique defensible key assigned
Review: Review only | Scored practice: No
Rationale: Region is not a top-level IAM statement element, but regional resource ARNs and Condition keys such as aws:RequestedRegion can play a role. The original claim that Region plays no role is too broad.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Study note. Action specifies the allowed DynamoDB operations.
B: Study note. Region is not a top-level policy element, but region conditions and regional ARNs can restrict access.
C: Study note. Effect determines whether the statement allows or denies.
D: Study note. Resource specifies DynamoDB table/index ARNs.

References:
AWS documentation: DynamoDB — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/amazondynamodb/latest/developerguide/Introduction.html
AWS documentation: IAM — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/IAM/latest/UserGuide/access_policies.html

You’ve configured CloudTrail to log all read- only data events. Which of the following events will CloudTrail log?

Uploading a file to an S3 bucket is a write- only event and hence would not be logged by the trail. Viewing an S3 bucket and creating a Lambda function are management events, not data events.
D: Incorrect for this question. Creating a function is a write management event.

CHAPTER 7: CloudTrail, CloudWatch, and AWS Config
Question 3 | Bank ID: Y07-003 | Task: 2.2
Sixty days ago, you created a trail in CloudTrail to log read- only management events. Subse- quently someone deleted the trail. Where can you look to find out who deleted it? No other trails are configured.
A. The IAM user log
B. The trail logs stored in S3
C. The CloudTrail event history in the region where the trail was configured
D. The CloudTrail event history in any region

Book answer: C
Practice answer: C
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: CloudTrail stores 90 days of event history for each region, regardless of whether a trail is configured. Event history is specific to the events occurring in that region. Because the trail was configured to log read-o nly management events, the trail logs would not contain a record of the trail’s deletion. They might contain a record of who viewed the trail, but that would be insufficient to establish who deleted it. There is no such thing as an IAM user log.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Incorrect for this question. There is no dedicated IAM user log for this API event.
B: Incorrect for this question. A read-only trail does not capture the DeleteTrail write event.
C: Correct. CloudTrail stores 90 days of event history for each region, regardless of whether a trail is configured. Event history is specific to the events occurring in that region. Because the trail was configured to log read-o nly management events, the trail logs would not contain a record of the trail’s deletion. They might contain a record of who viewed the trail, but that would be insufficient to establish who deleted it. There is no such thing as an IAM user log.
D: Incorrect for this question. Event history is regional rather than an unrestricted any-Region view.

References:
AWS documentation: CloudTrail — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/awscloudtrail/latest/userguide/cloudtrail-user-guide.html

Question 4 | Bank ID: Y07-004 | Task: 2.2
What uniquely distinguishes two CloudWatch metrics that have the same name and are in the same namespace?

C. The timestamp
D. The data point

Book answer: B
Practice answer: B

C: Incorrect for this question. A timestamp identifies a data point within a metric.
D: Incorrect for this question. A data point is a measurement rather than the metric's identity.

Which type of monitoring sends metrics to CloudWatch every five minutes?
A. Regular
B. Detailed
C. Basic
D. High resolution

Book answer: C
Practice answer: C
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: Basic monitoring sends metrics every five minutes, whereas detailed monitoring sends them every minute. CloudWatch can store metrics at regular or high resolution, but this affects how the metric is timestamped, rather than the frequency with which it’s delivered to CloudWatch.

B: Incorrect for this question. Detailed EC2 monitoring publishes at one-minute intervals.

D: Incorrect for this question. High-resolution custom metrics support finer-than-one-minute data.

Question 6 | Bank ID: Y07-006 | Task: 2.2
You update a custom CloudWatch metric with the timestamp of 15:57:08 and a value of 3. You then update the same metric with the timestamp of 15:57:37 and a value of 6. Assuming the metric is a high-r esolution metric, which of the following will CloudWatch do?
A. Record both values with the given timestamp.
B. Record the second value with the timestamp 15:57:37, overwriting the first value.
C. Record only the first value with the timestamp 15:57:08, ignoring the second value.
D. Record only the second value with the timestamp 15:57:00, overwriting the first value.

Book answer: A
Practice answer: A
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: High-resolution metrics retain sub-minute measurements at distinct timestamps. Ordinary-resolution measurements are aggregated according to requested statistics; they are not simply last-write-wins records.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Correct. High-resolution metrics retain sub-minute measurements at distinct timestamps. Ordinary-resolution measurements are aggregated according to requested statistics; they are not simply last-write-wins records.
B: Incorrect for this question. Different timestamps are retained rather than overwritten.
C: Incorrect for this question. The second measurement is not ignored.
D: Incorrect for this question. High-resolution data is not forced into a single minute's last value.

References:
AWS documentation: CloudTrail — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/awscloudtrail/latest/userguide/cloudtrail-user-guide.html

C: Incorrect for this question. A metric filter extracts metric values from log events.

Question 13 | Bank ID: Y07-013 | Task: 2.2
You want a CloudWatch alarm to change state when four consecutive evaluation periods elapse with no data. How should you configure the alarm to treat missing data?
A. As Missing
B. Breaching
C. Not Breaching
D. Ignore
E. As Not Missing

Book answer: B
Practice answer: B
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: Treat absent samples as Breaching to count them toward ALARM under the configured evaluation rules. Missing can produce INSUFFICIENT_DATA; Ignore preserves the current state and Not Breaching treats missing samples as healthy.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Incorrect for this question. Missing can leave the alarm in INSUFFICIENT_DATA.
B: Correct. Treat absent samples as Breaching to count them toward ALARM under the configured evaluation rules. Missing can produce INSUFFICIENT_DATA; Ignore preserves the current state and Not Breaching treats missing samples as healthy.
C: Incorrect for this question. Not Breaching treats absent samples as healthy.
D: Incorrect for this question. Ignore retains the prior state.
E: Incorrect for this question. Not Missing is not a supported treatment option.

References:
AWS documentation: CloudTrail — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/awscloudtrail/latest/userguide/cloudtrail-user-guide.html
AWS documentation: AWS Config — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/config/latest/developerguide/WhatIsConfig.html

Question 14 | Bank ID: Y07-014 | Task: 2.2
You’ve configured an alarm to monitor a metric in the AWS/EC2 namespace. You want CloudWatch to send you a text message and reboot an instance when an alarm is breaching. Which two actions should you configure in the alarm? (Choose two.)

D: Correct. CloudWatch can use the Simple Notification Service to send a text message. Cloud- Watch refers to this as a Notification action. To reboot an instance, you must use an EC2 action. The Auto Scaling action will not reboot an instance. SMS is not a valid CloudWatch alarm action.

CHAPTER 7: CloudTrail, CloudWatch, and AWS Config
Question 15 | Bank ID: Y07-015 | Task: 2.2
In a CloudWatch alarm, what does the EC2 recover action do to the monitored instance?
A. Migrates the instance to a different host
B. Reboots the instance
C. Deletes the instance and creates a new one
D. Restores the instance from a snapshot

Book answer: A
Practice answer: A
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: The recover action is useful when there’s a problem with an instance that requires AWS involvement to repair, such as a hardware failure. The recover action migrates the same instance to a new host. Rebooting an instance assumes the instance is running and entails the instance remaining on the same host. Recovering an instance does not involve restoring any data from a snapshot, as the instance retains the same EBS volume(s).

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Correct. The recover action is useful when there’s a problem with an instance that requires AWS involvement to repair, such as a hardware failure. The recover action migrates the same instance to a new host. Rebooting an instance assumes the instance is running and entails the instance remaining on the same host. Recovering an instance does not involve restoring any data from a snapshot, as the instance retains the same EBS volume(s).
B: Incorrect for this question. Reboot restarts the instance on its existing host rather than performs recovery migration.
C: Incorrect for this question. Recovery retains instance identity rather than creating a new instance.
D: Incorrect for this question. Recovery is not snapshot restoration.

References:
AWS documentation: EC2 — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AWSEC2/latest/UserGuide/concepts.html

Question 18 | Bank ID: Y07-018 | Task: 2.2
You configured AWS Config to monitor all your resources in the us- east- 1 region. After mak- ing several changes to the AWS resources in this region, you decided you want to delete the old configuration items. How can you accomplish this?
A. Pause the configuration recorder.
B. Delete the configuration recorder.
C. Delete the configuration snapshots.
D. Set the retention period to 30 days and wait for the configuration items to age out.

Book answer: D
Practice answer: D
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: You can’t delete configuration items manually, but you can have AWS Config delete them after no less than 30 days. Pausing or deleting the configuration recorder will stop AWS Con- fig from recording new changes but will not delete configuration items. Deleting configura- tion snapshots, which are objects stored in S3, will not delete the configuration items.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Incorrect for this question. Pausing stops new recording rather than removes stored history.
B: Incorrect for this question. Deleting the recorder does not clear previously stored history.
C: Incorrect for this question. Snapshot deletion does not remove AWS Config's retained configuration items.
D: Correct. You can’t delete configuration items manually, but you can have AWS Config delete them after no less than 30 days. Pausing or deleting the configuration recorder will stop AWS Con- fig from recording new changes but will not delete configuration items. Deleting configura- tion snapshots, which are objects stored in S3, will not delete the configuration items.

Question 19 | Bank ID: Y07-019 | Task: 2.2
Which of the following metric math expressions can CloudWatch graph? (Choose all that apply.)
A. AVG(m1)- m1
B. AVG(m1)
C. METRICS()/AVG(m1)
D. m1/m2

Book answer: C, D
Practice answer: A, C, D
Review: Corrected | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: AVG(m1) alone is a scalar. Subtracting a series from that scalar produces a series; METRICS()/AVG(m1) produces series, and m1/m2 produces a series. Thus A, C and D are graphable expressions. Practice answer key corrected from C, D to A, C, D.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Correct. AVG(m1) alone is a scalar. Subtracting a series from that scalar produces a series; METRICS()/AVG(m1) produces series, and m1/m2 produces a series. Thus A, C and D are graphable expressions.
B: Incorrect for this question. AVG of one series returns a scalar, which alone is not a graphable time series.
C: Correct. AVG(m1) alone is a scalar. Subtracting a series from that scalar produces a series; METRICS()/AVG(m1) produces series, and m1/m2 produces a series. Thus A, C and D are graphable expressions.
D: Correct. AVG(m1) alone is a scalar. Subtracting a series from that scalar produces a series; METRICS()/AVG(m1) produces series, and m1/m2 produces a series. Thus A, C and D are graphable expressions.

Question 1 | Bank ID: Y08-001 | Task: 3.4
Which of the following describes the function of a name server?
A. Translating human- readable domain names into IP addresses
B. Registering domain names with ICANN
C. Registering domain names with VeriSign
D. Applying routing policies to network packets

Book answer: A
Practice answer: A
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: Option A is the correct answer. Name servers resolve IP addresses from domain names, allowing clients to connect to resources. Domain registration is performed by domain name registrars. Routing policies are applied through record sets within hosted zones.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Correct. Option A is the correct answer. Name servers resolve IP addresses from domain names, allowing clients to connect to resources. Domain registration is performed by domain name registrars. Routing policies are applied through record sets within hosted zones.
B: Incorrect for this question. Registrars register domains; name servers answer DNS queries.
C: Incorrect for this question. Registry interaction is not the name server's resolution function.
D: Incorrect for this question. DNS answers influence destinations; name servers do not forward network packets.

Practice answer: A

Your web application is hosted within multiple AWS regions. Which of the following routing policies will ensure the fastest possible access for your users?
A. Latency
B. Weighted
C. Geolocation
D. Failover

Book answer: A
Practice answer: A
Review: Corrected | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: Latency-based routing uses AWS latency measurements to select the configured Region with the best latency estimate; DNS caching and actual network conditions still matter. Practice stem clarified; original retained below.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Correct. Latency routing selects a Region using measured network latency; no absolute fastest guarantee follows. Latency-based routing uses AWS latency measurements to select the configured Region with the best latency estimate; DNS caching and actual network conditions still matter.
B: Incorrect for this question. Weighted routing apportions responses by configured weights.
C: Incorrect for this question. Geolocation matches location rules rather than measures network latency.
D: Incorrect for this question. Failover prioritizes health of primary and secondary endpoints.

You’re testing three versions of a new application, with each version running on its own server and the current production version on a fourth server. You want to route 15 percent of your total traffic to each of the test servers and route the remaining 85 percent of traffic to the production server. Which routing policy will you use?
A. Failover
B. Weighted
C. Latency
D. Geolocation

Book answer: B
Practice answer: B
Review: Corrected | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: Weighted records can use weights 15, 15, 15 and 55. Weights distribute DNS responses approximately; resolver caching means they do not guarantee exact request counts. Practice stem clarified; original retained below.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Incorrect for this question. Failover chooses healthy primary/secondary endpoints rather than percentages.
B: Correct. Weighted routing fits proportional responses, but three times 15% plus 85% is impossible. Weighted records can use weights 15, 15, 15 and 55. Weights distribute DNS responses approximately; resolver caching means they do not guarantee exact request counts.
C: Incorrect for this question. Latency routing optimizes latency rather than fixed weights.
D: Incorrect for this question. Geolocation maps locations rather than target response proportions.

Practice wording:

A. Failover
B. Weighted
C. Latency
D. Geolocation

C: Incorrect for this question. Geolocation controls geography rather than failure-driven primary selection.
D: Correct. Use failover records under the user-facing name with primary and secondary endpoints. Failover incorporates backup resources for higher availability. Latency selects the avail- able resource with the lowest latency. Weighted policies route among multiple resources by percentage. Geolocation tailors request responses to the end user’s location.

B: Correct. To transfer a domain, you’ll need to make sure the domain isn’t set to locked. You’ll also need an authorization code that you’ll provide to Route 53. Copying name server addresses is necessary only for managing domains that are hosted on but not registered with Route 53. CNAME record sets are used to define one hostname as an alias for another.
C: Incorrect for this question. Changing delegation is for DNS hosting, not sufficient for transferring registration.
D: Incorrect for this question. A CNAME record does not transfer ownership between registrars.

References:
AWS documentation: Route 53 — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/Route53/latest/DeveloperGuide/Welcome.html

CHAPTER 8: DNS, Route 53 and CloudFront
Question 13 | Bank ID: Y08-013 | Task: 3.4
Which of the following actions will you need to perform to use Route 53 to manage a domain that’s being hosted on an external registrar?
A. Request an authorization code from the external registrar.
B. Copy the name server addresses from Route 53 to the external registrar admin page.
C. Create a hosted zone CNAME record set.
D. Unlock the domain transfer setting on the external registrar admin page.

Book answer: B
Practice answer: B
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: You can enable remotely registered domains on Route 53 by copying name server addresses into the remote registrar- provided interface (not the other way around). Making sure the domain isn’t set to locked and requesting authorization codes are used to transfer a domain to Route 53, not just to manage the routing. CNAME record sets are used to define one hostname as an alias for another.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Incorrect for this question. Authorization codes are for transferring registration.
B: Correct. You can enable remotely registered domains on Route 53 by copying name server addresses into the remote registrar- provided interface (not the other way around). Making sure the domain isn’t set to locked and requesting authorization codes are used to transfer a domain to Route 53, not just to manage the routing. CNAME record sets are used to define one hostname as an alias for another.
C: Incorrect for this question. CNAME does not delegate the domain's DNS authority.
D: Incorrect for this question. Transfer unlock is not necessary merely to change name server delegation.

References:
AWS documentation: Route 53 — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/Route53/latest/DeveloperGuide/Welcome.html

CHAPTER 8: DNS, Route 53 and CloudFront
Question 14 | Bank ID: Y08-014 | Task: 3.4
Your multiserver application has been generating quality- related complaints from users and your logs show some servers are underused and others have been experiencing intermittent failures. How do Route 53 health checks test for the health of a resource so that a failover policy can direct your users appropriately?
A. It periodically tries to load the index.php page.
B. It periodically tries to load the index.html page.
C. It periodically tries to load a specified web page.
D. It periodically tries to log into the resource using SSH.

Book answer: C
Practice answer: C
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: You specify the web page that you want used for testing when you configure your health check. There is no default page. Remote SSH sessions would be impossible for a number of reasons and wouldn’t definitively confirm a running resource in any case.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Incorrect for this question. index.php is only checked if configured as the path.
B: Incorrect for this question. index.html is only checked if configured as the path.
C: Correct. For an HTTP/HTTPS endpoint check, Route 53 requests the configured path. You specify the web page that you want used for testing when you configure your health check. There is no default page. Remote SSH sessions would be impossible for a number of reasons and wouldn’t definitively confirm a running resource in any case.
D: Incorrect for this question. SSH login is not a Route 53 endpoint health-check protocol.

References:
AWS documentation: Route 53 — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/Route53/latest/DeveloperGuide/Welcome.html

Question 15 | Bank ID: Y08-015 | Task: 3.4
Which of the following most accurately describes the difference between geolocation and geoproximity routing policies?
A. Geoproximity policies specify geographic areas by their relationship either to a particular longitude and latitude or to an AWS region, whereas geolocation policies use the continent, country, or U.S. state where the request originated to decide what resource to send.
B. Geolocation policies specify geographic areas by their relationship either to a particular longitude and latitude or to an AWS region, whereas geoproximity policies use the conti- nent, country, or U.S. state where the request originated to decide what resource to send.
C. Geolocation policies will direct traffic to the resource you identify as primary as long as health checks confirm that that resource is running properly, whereas geoproximity pol- icies allow you to deliver web pages in customer- appropriate languages.
D. Geolocation policies use a health check configuration routing to make a deployment more highly available, whereas geoproximity policies leverage resources running in mul- tiple AWS regions to provide service to clients from the instances that will deliver the best experience.

Book answer: A
Practice answer: A
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: Geolocation selects records by continent, country or US state. Geoproximity compares user/resource geography and can apply bias to shift routing boundaries; it describes resource location rather than merely pinpointing users.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Correct. Geolocation selects records by continent, country or US state. Geoproximity compares user/resource geography and can apply bias to shift routing boundaries; it describes resource location rather than merely pinpointing users.
B: Incorrect for this question. This reverses geolocation and geoproximity.
C: Incorrect for this question. Primary health preference describes failover; language steering is associated with geolocation.
D: Incorrect for this question. This does not identify the location-category versus distance/bias distinction.

CHAPTER 8: DNS, Route 53 and CloudFront
Question 18 | Bank ID: Y08-018 | Task: 3.4
What’s the best way to control the costs your CloudFront distribution incurs?
A. Select a price class that maintains copies in only a limited subset of CloudFront’s edge locations.
B. Configure a custom SSL certificate to restrict access to HTTPS requests only.
C. Disable the use of Alternate Domain Names (CNAMES) for your distribution.
D. Enable Compress Objects Automatically for your distribution.

Book answer: A
Practice answer: No unique defensible key assigned
Review: Review only | Scored practice: No
Rationale: Price classes limit edge-location coverage and compression reduces transferred bytes. Both can reduce costs. The best choice depends on viewer locations, object compressibility and the traffic profile.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Study note. Price classes constrain costly geographic edge coverage.
B: Study note. A custom TLS certificate does not select cheaper edge coverage.
C: Study note. Removing alternate names does not directly optimize distribution delivery cost.
D: Study note. Compression also reduces transferred bytes and cost; best depends on the workload.

References:
AWS documentation: CloudFront — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonCloudFront/latest/DeveloperGuide/Introduction.html

CHAPTER 8: DNS, Route 53 and CloudFront
Question 19 | Bank ID: Y08-019 | Task: 3.4
Which of the following is not necessarily a direct benefit of using a CloudFront distribution?
A. User requests from an edge location that’s recently received the same request will be delivered with lower latency.
B. CloudFront distributions can be directly mapped to Route 53 hosted zones.
C. All user requests will be delivered with lower latency.
D. You can incorporate free encryption certificates into your infrastructure.

Book answer: C
Practice answer: C
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: Not every CloudFront distribution is optimized for low- latency service. Requests of an edge location will only achieve lower latency after copies of your origin files are already cached. Therefore, a response to the first request might not be fast because CloudFront still has to copy the file from the origin server.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Incorrect for this question. Cache hits near viewers generally reduce latency.
B: Incorrect for this question. DNS can map a hostname to a distribution, although hosted zones are not directly mapped as objects.
C: Correct. Not every CloudFront distribution is optimized for low- latency service. Requests of an edge location will only achieve lower latency after copies of your origin files are already cached. Therefore, a response to the first request might not be fast because CloudFront still has to copy the file from the origin server.
D: Incorrect for this question. ACM can provide certificates for compatible CloudFront deployments.

References:
AWS documentation: CloudFront — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonCloudFront/latest/DeveloperGuide/Introduction.html

Question 1 | Bank ID: Y09-001 | Task: 3.5
What are valid use cases for transforming data when importing it into a data lake? (Select three.)
A. Imposing consistent timestamps
B. Removing corrupted data
C. Creating a schema
D. Removing duplicate data
E. Visualizing data

Book answer: A, B, D
Practice answer: A, B, D
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: Data transformation includes changing the contents or format of data. Creating a schema is something you do with structured data, and a data lake doesn’t impose or require a schema. Visualizing data is something that occurs with data that’s already stored in the data lake.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Correct. Data transformation includes changing the contents or format of data. Creating a schema is something you do with structured data, and a data lake doesn’t impose or require a schema. Visualizing data is something that occurs with data that’s already stored in the data lake.
B: Correct. Data transformation includes changing the contents or format of data. Creating a schema is something you do with structured data, and a data lake doesn’t impose or require a schema. Visualizing data is something that occurs with data that’s already stored in the data lake.
C: Incorrect for this question. Schema/catalog creation is metadata organization rather than the requested cleanup operation.
D: Correct. Data transformation includes changing the contents or format of data. Creating a schema is something you do with structured data, and a data lake doesn’t impose or require a schema. Visualizing data is something that occurs with data that’s already stored in the data lake.
E: Incorrect for this question. Visualization analyzes/presents data rather than cleans imported records.

References:
AWS documentation: Glue — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/glue/latest/dg/what-is-glue.html

Question 13 | Bank ID: Y09-013 | Task: 3.5
Which of the following Kinesis services requires you to specify a destination for the stream?

A: Incorrect for this question. Video Streams stores media rather than requires a Firehose-style delivery destination.
B: Incorrect for this question. Data Streams stores records for consumers rather than requires a configured destination.
C: Correct. Kinesis Data Firehose requires you to specify a destination for a delivery stream. Kinesis Video Streams and Kinesis Data Streams use a producer- consumer model that allows con- sumers to subscribe to a stream. There is no such thing as Kinesis Data Warehouse.
D: Incorrect for this question. Kinesis Data Warehouse is not a named service.

When deciding whether to use SQS or Kinesis Data Streams to ingest data, which of the following should you take into account?
A. The frequency of data
B. The total amount of data
C. The number of consumers that need to receive the data
D. The order of data

Book answer: C
Practice answer: No unique defensible key assigned
Review: Review only | Scored practice: No
Rationale: Independent replaying consumers distinguish Kinesis from work-distributing SQS, but ordering, volume, latency and message semantics also matter. The broad consideration question has multiple valid factors.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Study note. Frequency matters for sizing and latency, so it is not wholly irrelevant.
B: Study note. Data volume also affects capacity and cost.
C: Study note. Independent consumers can replay a Kinesis stream; SQS normally distributes work among consumers.
D: Study note. Ordering also matters; standard SQS is unordered, FIFO and Kinesis have scoped ordering.

B. 2
C. 4
D. 8

Book answer: B
Practice answer: B
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: Shards determine the capacity of a Kinesis Data Stream. A single shard gives you writes of up to 1 MB per second, so you’d need two shards to get 2 MB of throughput.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Incorrect for this question. A provisioned shard accepts up to 1 MiB/s or 1,000 records/s.
B: Correct. Two shards meet 2 MiB/s if the record-count limit and partition distribution also fit. Shards determine the capacity of a Kinesis Data Stream. A single shard gives you writes of up to 1 MB per second, so you’d need two shards to get 2 MB of throughput.
C: Incorrect for this question. Four exceeds the throughput-only minimum.
D: Incorrect for this question. Eight exceeds the throughput-only minimum.

Question 1 | Bank ID: Y10-001 | Task: 2.2
What’s the minimum level of availability you need to stay under 30 minutes of downtime per month?
A. 99 percent
B. 99.9 percent
C. 99.95 percent
D. 99.999 percent

Book answer: C
Practice answer: C
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: Availability of 99.95 percent translates to about 22 minutes of downtime per month, or 4 hours and 23 minutes per year. Availability of 99.999 percent is less than 30 seconds of downtime per month, but the question calls for the minimum level of availability. Availability of 99 percent yields more than 7 hours of downtime per month, whereas 99.9 percent is more than 43 minutes of downtime per month.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Incorrect for this question. 99% allows about 432 minutes in a 30-day month.
B: Incorrect for this question. 99.9% allows about 43.2 minutes in a 30-day month.
C: Correct. Availability of 99.95 percent translates to about 22 minutes of downtime per month, or 4 hours and 23 minutes per year. Availability of 99.999 percent is less than 30 seconds of downtime per month, but the question calls for the minimum level of availability. Availability of 99 percent yields more than 7 hours of downtime per month, whereas 99.9 percent is more than 43 minutes of downtime per month.
D: Incorrect for this question. 99.999% meets the goal but is not the lowest offered adequate level.

Question 2 | Bank ID: Y10-002 | Task: 2.2
Your application runs on two EC2 instances in one availability zone. An elastic load balancer distributes user traffic evenly across the healthy instances. The application on each instance connects to a single RDS database instance. Assuming each EC2 instance has an availability of 90 percent and the RDS instance has an availability of 95 percent, what is the total appli- cation availability?
A. 94.05 percent
B. 99 percent
C. 99.9 percent
D. 99.95 percent

Book answer: A
Practice answer: A
Review: Corrected | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: Assuming independent compute failures, the parallel tier has 1 - (1 - 0.90)^2 = 0.99 availability. Multiplying by the required database availability gives 0.99 times 0.95 = 0.9405. Shared-AZ failures violate independence, so this is illustrative mathematics.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Correct. Assuming independent instance failures: (1 - 0.1 squared) times 0.95 = 94.05%. Assuming independent compute failures, the parallel tier has 1 - (1 - 0.90)^2 = 0.99 availability. Multiplying by the required database availability gives 0.99 times 0.95 = 0.9405. Shared-AZ failures violate independence, so this is illustrative mathematics.
B: Incorrect for this question. 99% describes only the parallel compute tier under independence.
C: Incorrect for this question. This ignores the database's 95% availability bottleneck.
D: Incorrect for this question. This exceeds the stated component availability calculation.

References:
AWS documentation: EC2 — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AWSEC2/latest/UserGuide/concepts.html
AWS documentation: RDS — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/Welcome.html
