Question 6 | Bank ID: Y10-006 | Task: 2.2
You create an Auto Scaling group with a minimum group size of 3, a maximum group size of 10, and a desired capacity of 5. You then manually terminate two instances in the group. Which of the following will Auto Scaling do?
A. Create two new instances.
B. Reduce the desired capacity to 3.
C. Nothing.
D. Increment the minimum group size to 5.

Book answer: A
Practice answer: A
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: No
Rationale: Auto Scaling strives to maintain the number of instances specified in the desired capacity setting. If the desired capacity setting isn’t set, Auto Scaling will attempt to maintain the number of instances specified by the minimum group size. Given a desired capacity of 5, there should be five healthy instances. If you manually terminate two of them, Auto Scaling will create two new ones to replace them. Auto Scaling will not adjust the desired capacity or minimum group size. Exact duplicate of Y02-021; retained for study, excluded from scored selection.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Correct. Auto Scaling strives to maintain the number of instances specified in the desired capacity setting. If the desired capacity setting isn’t set, Auto Scaling will attempt to maintain the number of instances specified by the minimum group size. Given a desired capacity of 5, there should be five healthy instances. If you manually terminate two of them, Auto Scaling will create two new ones to replace them. Auto Scaling will not adjust the desired capacity or minimum group size.
B: Incorrect for this question. Manual EC2 termination does not lower desired capacity.
C: Incorrect for this question. The group restores desired capacity of five.
D: Incorrect for this question. The minimum is not automatically raised by manual termination.

Practice wording:
You create an Auto Scaling group with a minimum group size of 3, a maximum group size of 10, and a desired capacity of 5. You then manually terminate two instances in the group. Which of the following will Auto Scaling do?
A. Create two new instances.
B. Reduce the desired capacity to 3.
C. Nothing.
D. Increment the minimum group size to 5.

Question 12 | Bank ID: Y10-012 | Task: 2.2
You’re running an RDS instance in one availability zone. What should you implement to be able to achieve a recovery point objective (RPO) of five minutes?
A. Configure multi- AZ.
B. Enable automated snapshots.
C. Add a read replica in the same region.
D. Add a read replica in a different region.

Book answer: B
Practice answer: No unique defensible key assigned
Review: Review only | Scored practice: No
Rationale: Automated backups plus transaction logs enable PITR near the latest restorable time; snapshots alone do not give a five-minute RPO. Multi-AZ and replica strategies address different failures and can also reduce data loss, so failure scope is necessary.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Study note. Multi-AZ standby replication also gives very low data loss for instance/AZ failures.
B: Study note. Automated backups with transaction logs allow point-in-time restoration, unlike daily snapshots alone.
C: Study note. A read replica can also have low lag, but asynchronous lag is not a guaranteed RPO.
D: Study note. Cross-Region replica lag also depends on asynchronous replication and workload.

Question 13 | Bank ID: Y10-013 | Task: 2.2
When creating subnets in a VPC, what are two reasons to leave sufficient space in the VPC for more subnets later? (Choose two.)

B. You may need to implement RDS.

Practice answer: No unique defensible key assigned
Review: Review only | Scored practice: No
Rationale: Leaving subnet space supports future tiers, new AZs and database subnet-group needs. A, B and C can all be reasonable, so the required two-choice key lacks distinguishing requirements.

B: Study note. RDS often requires subnet groups across AZs, so this is also a valid planning consideration.
C: Study note. Leaving subnet space supports future tiers, new AZs and database subnet-group needs. A, B and C can all be reasonable, so the required two-choice key lacks distinguishing requirements.

Question 14 | Bank ID: Y10-014 | Task: 2.2
You plan to deploy 50 EC2 instances, each with two private IP addresses. To put all of these instances in a single subnet, which subnet CIDRs could you use? (Choose all that apply.)

Book answer: A, D

Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes

Question 18 | Bank ID: Y10-018 | Task: 2.2

A. 3
B. 16
C. 8
D. 4

Book answer: D
Practice answer: D
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: By running four instances in each zone, you have a total of 12 instances in the region. If one zone fails, you lose four of those instances and are left with eight. Running eight or 16 instances in each zone would allow you to withstand one zone failure, but the question asks for the minimum number of instances. Three instances per zone would give you nine total in the region, but if one zone fails, you’d be left with only six.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Incorrect for this question. After losing a zone, two times three leaves only six instances.
B: Incorrect for this question. Sixteen per zone far exceeds the eight-instance remaining requirement.
C: Incorrect for this question. Eight per zone provides more capacity than the minimum.
D: Correct. By running four instances in each zone, you have a total of 12 instances in the region. If one zone fails, you lose four of those instances and are left with eight. Running eight or 16 instances in each zone would allow you to withstand one zone failure, but the question asks for the minimum number of instances. Three instances per zone would give you nine total in the region, but if one zone fails, you’d be left with only six.

Question 20 | Bank ID: Y10-020 | Task: 2.2
You’re running an application in two regions and using multi- AZ RDS with read replicas in both regions. Users normally access the application in only one region by browsing to a public domain name that resolves to an elastic load balancer. If that region fails, which of the following should you do to fail over to the other region? (Choose all that apply.)
A. Update the DNS record to point to the load balancer in the other region.
B. Point the load balancer to the other region.
C. Fail over to the database in the other region.
D. Restore the database from a snapshot.

Book answer: A, C
Practice answer: A, C
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: Route the user-facing DNS name to the secondary Region load balancer, promote/select the secondary database and repoint the application connection. Replication lag and readiness determine recovery; changing a primary load balancer does not itself perform this complete failover.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Correct. Route the user-facing DNS name to the secondary Region load balancer, promote/select the secondary database and repoint the application connection. Replication lag and readiness determine recovery; changing a primary load balancer does not itself perform this complete failover.
B: Incorrect for this question. A regional load balancer cannot simply be moved to another Region.
C: Correct. Route the user-facing DNS name to the secondary Region load balancer, promote/select the secondary database and repoint the application connection. Replication lag and readiness determine recovery; changing a primary load balancer does not itself perform this complete failover.
D: Incorrect for this question. Snapshot restore may be a fallback, but existing replicated data is the described recovery path.

References:
AWS documentation: RDS — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/Welcome.html

Question 21 | Bank ID: Y10-021 | Task: 2.1
When a consumer grabs a message from an SQS queue, what happens to the message? (Choose two.)
A. It is immediately deleted from the queue.
B. It remains in the queue for 30 seconds and is then deleted.
C. It stays in the queue for the remaining duration of the retention period.
D. It becomes invisible to other consumers for the duration of the visibility timeout.

Book answer: C, D
Practice answer: C, D
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: After a consumer grabs a message, the message is not deleted. Instead, the message becomes invisible to other consumers for the duration of the visibility timeout. The message is automatically deleted from the queue after it’s been in there for the duration of the reten- tion period.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Incorrect for this question. Receipt does not delete the message; the consumer must acknowledge with DeleteMessage.
B: Incorrect for this question. Visibility expiration makes it visible again rather than deletes it.
C: Correct. The message remains until explicitly deleted or retention expires. After a consumer grabs a message, the message is not deleted. Instead, the message becomes invisible to other consumers for the duration of the visibility timeout. The message is automatically deleted from the queue after it’s been in there for the duration of the reten- tion period.
D: Correct. After a consumer grabs a message, the message is not deleted. Instead, the message becomes invisible to other consumers for the duration of the visibility timeout. The message is automatically deleted from the queue after it’s been in there for the duration of the reten- tion period.

Question 29 | Bank ID: Y10-029 | Task: 2.1
Which of the following situations calls for a dead- letter queue?
A. A message sits in the queue for too long and gets deleted.
B. Different consumers receive and process the same message.
C. Messages are mysteriously disappearing from the queue.
D. A consumer repeatedly fails to process a particular message.

Book answer: D
Practice answer: D
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: Dead- letter queues are for messages that a consumer is unable to process. To use a dead- letter queue, you create a queue of the same type as the source queue and set the maxReceiveCount to the maximum number of times a message can be received before it’s moved to the dead- letter queue.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Incorrect for this question. Retention expiry deletes data; it does not trigger redrive.
B: Incorrect for this question. Duplicate processing calls for idempotency rather than necessarily a DLQ.
C: Incorrect for this question. Unexpected disappearance requires investigation rather than a DLQ assumption.
D: Correct. Dead- letter queues are for messages that a consumer is unable to process. To use a dead- letter queue, you create a queue of the same type as the source queue and set the maxReceiveCount to the maximum number of times a message can be received before it’s moved to the dead- letter queue.

AWS documentation: EC2 Auto Scaling — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/autoscaling/ec2/userguide/what-is-amazon-ec2-auto-scaling.html

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Correct. Elastic Container Service is a good platform for microservices. Lambda functions execu- tions are short- lived (having a 15-m inute maximum) and wouldn’t work well for this kind of deployment. Beanstalk operations aren’t ideal for microservices. ECR is a repository for con- tainer images and isn’t a deployment platform on its own.
B: Incorrect for this question. Lambda can implement suitable event-driven microservices, but this stem focuses on server/container components.
C: Incorrect for this question. ECR stores container images rather than runs them.
D: Incorrect for this question. Beanstalk manages application environments but is less directly aligned with container task orchestration.

References:
AWS documentation: EC2 — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AWSEC2/latest/UserGuide/concepts.html

Book answer: C

D: Study note. Transfer Acceleration accelerates transfers rather than creates a recovery replica.

Book answer: C, D

Organizing stacks by resource cost doesn’t offer any advantage since the cost is the same regardless of which stack a resource is in.

Organizing stacks by resource cost doesn’t offer any advantage since the cost is the same regardless of which stack a resource is in.

Question 1 | Bank ID: Y12-001 | Task: 1.2
Which of the following options can you not set in a password policy? (Choose two.)

B. Require the use of numbers

Book answer: A, C
Practice answer: A, C

Rationale: A password policy can specify a minimum password length but not a maximum. It can prevent a user from reusing a password they used before but not one that another user has used. A password policy can require a password to contain numbers. It can also require administrator approval to reset an expired password.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Correct. A password policy can specify a minimum password length but not a maximum. It can prevent a user from reusing a password they used before but not one that another user has used. A password policy can require a password to contain numbers. It can also require administrator approval to reset an expired password.
B: Incorrect for this question. Requiring numbers is supported.
C: Correct. A password policy can specify a minimum password length but not a maximum. It can prevent a user from reusing a password they used before but not one that another user has used. A password policy can require a password to contain numbers. It can also require administrator approval to reset an expired password.
D: Incorrect for this question. Requiring an administrator reset after expiry is supported.

CHAPTER 12: Secure Architectures
Question 2 | Bank ID: Y12-002 | Task: 1.2
An IAM user is attached to a customer- managed policy granting them sufficient access to carry out their duties. You want to require multifactor authentication (MFA) for this user to use the AWS CLI. What element should you change in the policy?
A. Resource
B. Condition
C. Action
D. Principal

Book answer: B
Practice answer: B
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: The Condition element lets you require MFA to grant the permissions defined in the policy. The Resource and Action elements define what those permissions are but not the con- ditions under which those permissions are granted. The Principal element is not used in an identity- based policy.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Incorrect for this question. Resource selects affected ARNs rather than the authentication requirement.
B: Correct. The Condition element lets you require MFA to grant the permissions defined in the policy. The Resource and Action elements define what those permissions are but not the con- ditions under which those permissions are granted. The Principal element is not used in an identity- based policy.
C: Incorrect for this question. Action selects API operations rather than whether MFA was used.
D: Incorrect for this question. Principal is not normally an element of an identity-based user permissions policy.

References:
AWS documentation: IAM — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/IAM/latest/UserGuide/access_policies.html

Question 3 | Bank ID: Y12-003 | Task: 1.2
You created an IAM policy that another administrator subsequently modified. You need to restore the policy to its original state but don’t remember how it was configured. What should you do to restore the policy? (Choose two.)
A. Consult CloudTrail global management event logs.
B. Restore the policy from a snapshot.
C. Consult CloudTrail data event logs.
D. Revert to the previous policy version.

Book answer: A, D
Practice answer: A, D
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: IAM keeps five versions of every customer- managed policy. When CloudTrail is config- ured to log global management events, it will record any policy changes in the request param- eters of the CreatePolicyVersion operation. There is no such thing as a policy snapshot. CloudTrail data event logs will not log IAM events.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Correct. CloudTrail management events can help identify policy edits and prior versions. IAM keeps five versions of every customer- managed policy. When CloudTrail is config- ured to log global management events, it will record any policy changes in the request param- eters of the CreatePolicyVersion operation. There is no such thing as a policy snapshot. CloudTrail data event logs will not log IAM events.
B: Incorrect for this question. IAM policies do not have a generic snapshot restore feature.
C: Incorrect for this question. IAM policy changes are management rather than resource data events.
D: Correct. IAM keeps five versions of every customer- managed policy. When CloudTrail is config- ured to log global management events, it will record any policy changes in the request param- eters of the CreatePolicyVersion operation. There is no such thing as a policy snapshot. CloudTrail data event logs will not log IAM events.

References:
AWS documentation: IAM — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/IAM/latest/UserGuide/access_policies.html

CHAPTER 12: Secure Architectures
Question 4 | Bank ID: Y12-004 | Task: 1.2
An IAM user with full access to all EC2 actions in all regions assumes a role that has access to only the EC2 RunInstances operation in the us- east- 1 region. What will the user be able to do under the assumed role?
A. Create a new instance in any region.
B. Create a new instance in the us- east- 1 region.
C. Start an existing instance in the us- east- 1 region.
D. Start an existing instance in any region.

Book answer: B
Practice answer: B
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: When an IAM user assumes a role, the user gains the permissions assigned to that role but loses the permissions assigned to the IAM user. The RunInstances action launches a new instance. Because the role can perform the RunInstances action in the us- east- 1 region, the user, upon assuming the role, can create a new instance in the us- east- 1 region but cannot perform any other actions. StartInstances starts an existing instance but doesn’t launch a new one.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Incorrect for this question. The role limits this operation to us-east-1.
B: Correct. When an IAM user assumes a role, the user gains the permissions assigned to that role but loses the permissions assigned to the IAM user. The RunInstances action launches a new instance. Because the role can perform the RunInstances action in the us- east- 1 region, the user, upon assuming the role, can create a new instance in the us- east- 1 region but cannot perform any other actions. StartInstances starts an existing instance but doesn’t launch a new one.
C: Incorrect for this question. RunInstances does not authorize StartInstances.
D: Incorrect for this question. The role does not inherit the user's original broader EC2 permissions.

References:
AWS documentation: EC2 — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AWSEC2/latest/UserGuide/concepts.html
AWS documentation: IAM — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/IAM/latest/UserGuide/access_policies.html

Question 5 | Bank ID: Y12-005 | Task: 1.3
Several objects in a S3 bucket are encrypted using a KMS customer master key. Which of the following will give an IAM user permission to decrypt these objects?
A. Add the user to the key policy as a key user.
B. Grant the user access to the key using an IAM policy.
C. Add the user to the key policy as a key administrator.
D. Add the user as a principal to the bucket policy.

Book answer: A
Practice answer: No unique defensible key assigned
Review: Review only | Scored practice: No
Rationale: Decryption requires S3 read permission plus KMS authorization. A key policy can grant key use directly, or delegate permissions to IAM policies. With an unspecified key policy, A and B are not mutually exclusive mechanisms.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Study note. Key-user permissions can authorize decryption, but S3 read permission is also necessary.
B: Study note. IAM policies can authorize decrypt when the key policy enables delegation.
C: Study note. Key administration does not automatically grant key-use permissions.
D: Study note. Bucket access alone does not authorize KMS decryption.

References:
AWS documentation: IAM — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/IAM/latest/UserGuide/access_policies.html
AWS documentation: KMS — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/kms/latest/developerguide/overview.html
AWS documentation: S3 — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonS3/latest/userguide/Welcome.html

Question 6 | Bank ID: Y12-006 | Task: 1.2
You run a public- facing application on EC2 instances. The application is backed by a data- base running on RDS. Users access it using multiple domain names that are hosted in Route 53. You want to get an idea of what IP addresses are accessing your application. Which of the following would you stream to CloudWatch Logs to get this information?
A. RDS logs
B. DNS query logs
C. VPC flow logs
D. CloudTrail logs

Book answer: C
Practice answer: C
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: VPC flow logs record source IP address information for traffic coming into your VPC. DNS query logs record the IP addresses of DNS queries, but those won’t necessarily be the same IP addresses accessing your application. Because users won’t directly connect to your RDS instance, RDS logs won’t record their IP addresses. CloudTrail logs can record the source IP address of API requests but not connections to an EC2 instance.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Incorrect for this question. RDS logs concern database clients rather than incoming application web traffic.
B: Incorrect for this question. DNS resolver IPs need not equal the actual application clients.
C: Correct. VPC flow logs record source IP address information for traffic coming into your VPC. DNS query logs record the IP addresses of DNS queries, but those won’t necessarily be the same IP addresses accessing your application. Because users won’t directly connect to your RDS instance, RDS logs won’t record their IP addresses. CloudTrail logs can record the source IP address of API requests but not connections to an EC2 instance.
D: Incorrect for this question. CloudTrail tracks AWS API calls rather than ordinary HTTP connections to EC2.

References:
AWS documentation: Route 53 — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/Route53/latest/DeveloperGuide/Welcome.html
AWS documentation: EC2 — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AWSEC2/latest/UserGuide/concepts.html
AWS documentation: RDS — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonRDS/latest/UserGuide/Welcome.html

Question 7 | Bank ID: Y12-007 | Task: 1.2
You’re running a web server that keeps a detailed log of web requests. You want to determine which IP address has made the most requests in the last 24 hours. What should you do to accomplish this? (Choose two.)
A. Create a metric filter.
B. Stream the web server logs to CloudWatch Logs.
C. Upload the web server log to S3.
D. Use Athena to query the data.

Book answer: C, D
Practice answer: C, D
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: Athena lets you perform advanced SQL queries against data stored in S3. A metric filter can increment based on the occurrence of a value in a CloudWatch log group but can’t tell you the most frequently occurring IP address.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Incorrect for this question. A metric filter is not the most direct retrospective top-IP query method.
B: Incorrect for this question. CloudWatch Logs with Logs Insights can also solve this; B alone does not identify a query step.
C: Correct. Athena lets you perform advanced SQL queries against data stored in S3. A metric filter can increment based on the occurrence of a value in a CloudWatch log group but can’t tell you the most frequently occurring IP address.
D: Correct. Athena lets you perform advanced SQL queries against data stored in S3. A metric filter can increment based on the occurrence of a value in a CloudWatch log group but can’t tell you the most frequently occurring IP address.

Question 8 | Bank ID: Y12-008 | Task: 1.2
An application running on an EC2 instance has been updated to send large amounts of data to a server in your datacenter for backup. Previously, the instance generated very little traffic. Which GuardDuty finding type is this likely to trigger?
A. Behavior
B. Backdoor
C. Stealth
D. ResourceConsumption

Book answer: A
Practice answer: No unique defensible key assigned
Review: Review only | Scored practice: No
Rationale: Unusual outbound traffic can trigger GuardDuty anomaly findings, but the exact current finding depends on destination, volume, service and threat evidence. Historical generic category names do not uniquely diagnose an authorized backup.

A: Study note. Anomalous traffic can produce behavior-related findings, but exact detection depends on the activity.
B: Study note. Backdoor findings concern malicious command/control activity.
C: Study note. Stealth findings concern evasion patterns rather than just increased backup traffic.
D: Study note. Increased traffic alone does not define this named category unambiguously.

Question 9 | Bank ID: Y12-009 | Task: 1.2
You’ve set up an AWS Config managed rule to check whether a particular security group is attached to every instance in a VPC. You receive an SNS notification that an instance is out of compliance. But when you check the instance a few hours later, the security group is attached. Which of the following may help explain the apparent discrepancy? (Choose two.)
A. The AWS Config timeline
B. Lambda logs
C. CloudTrail management event logs
D. VPC flow logs

Book answer: A, C
Practice answer: A, C
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: The AWS Config timeline will show every configuration change that occurred on the instance, including the attachment and detachment of security groups. CloudTrail management event logs will also show the actions that detached and attached the secu- rity group. Although AWS Config rules use Lambda functions, the Lambda logs for AWS managed rules are not available to you. VPC flow logs capture traffic ingressing a VPC, but not API events.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Correct. The AWS Config timeline will show every configuration change that occurred on the instance, including the attachment and detachment of security groups. CloudTrail management event logs will also show the actions that detached and attached the secu- rity group. Although AWS Config rules use Lambda functions, the Lambda logs for AWS managed rules are not available to you. VPC flow logs capture traffic ingressing a VPC, but not API events.
B: Incorrect for this question. Lambda logs help only if a remediation function was actually involved.
C: Correct. The AWS Config timeline will show every configuration change that occurred on the instance, including the attachment and detachment of security groups. CloudTrail management event logs will also show the actions that detached and attached the secu- rity group. Although AWS Config rules use Lambda functions, the Lambda logs for AWS managed rules are not available to you. VPC flow logs capture traffic ingressing a VPC, but not API events.
D: Incorrect for this question. Flow logs capture connections rather than security-group association changes.

Question 11 | Bank ID: Y12-011 | Task: 1.2
You have a distributed application running in datacenters around the world. The applica- tion connects to a public Simple Queue Service (SQS) endpoint to send messages to a queue. How can you prevent an attacker from using this endpoint to gain unauthorized access to the queue? (Choose two.)
A. Network access control lists
B. Security groups
C. IAM policies
D. SQS access policies

Book answer: C, D
Practice answer: C, D
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: You can use an IAM policy or SQS access policy to restrict queue access to certain principals or those coming from a specified IP range. You cannot use network access control lists or security groups to restrict access to a public endpoint.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Incorrect for this question. Subnet NACLs do not attach to public SQS endpoints.
B: Incorrect for this question. Security groups do not attach directly to a public SQS queue.
C: Correct. You can use an IAM policy or SQS access policy to restrict queue access to certain principals or those coming from a specified IP range. You cannot use network access control lists or security groups to restrict access to a public endpoint.
D: Correct. You can use an IAM policy or SQS access policy to restrict queue access to certain principals or those coming from a specified IP range. You cannot use network access control lists or security groups to restrict access to a public endpoint.

Question 12 | Bank ID: Y12-012 | Task: 1.2
You’re using a public-f acing application load balancer to forward traffic to EC2 instances in an Auto Scaling group. What can you do to ensure users on the Internet can reach the load balancer over HTTPS without reaching your instances directly? (Choose two.)
A. Create a security group that allows all inbound traffic to TCP port 443.
B. Attach the security group to the instances.
C. Attach the security group to the load balancer.
D. Remove the Internet gateway from the VPC.
E. Create a security group that allows all inbound traffic to TCP port 80.

Book answer: A, C
Practice answer: A, C
Review: Corrected | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: Allow inbound TCP 443 in the load-balancer security group and attach it to the ALB. Also configure an HTTPS listener/certificate and target rules allowing only the ALB group. The selected pair alone does not isolate targets. Practice stem clarified; original retained below.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Correct. Allow inbound TCP 443 in the load-balancer security group and attach it to the ALB. Also configure an HTTPS listener/certificate and target rules allowing only the ALB group. The selected pair alone does not isolate targets.
B: Incorrect for this question. Allowing public HTTPS directly on the instances conflicts with the intended isolation.
C: Correct. Allow inbound TCP 443 in the load-balancer security group and attach it to the ALB. Also configure an HTTPS listener/certificate and target rules allowing only the ALB group. The selected pair alone does not isolate targets.
D: Incorrect for this question. Removing the Internet gateway would make the Internet-facing load balancer unreachable.
E: Incorrect for this question. Port 80 does not meet the public HTTPS listener requirement.

Practice wording:
Which two steps expose an application load balancer over HTTPS? Separately, restrict the private targets to traffic from the load-balancer security group.
A. Create a security group that allows all inbound traffic to TCP port 443.
B. Attach the security group to the instances.
C. Attach the security group to the load balancer.
D. Remove the Internet gateway from the VPC.
E. Create a security group that allows all inbound traffic to TCP port 80.

References:
AWS documentation: IAM — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/IAM/latest/UserGuide/access_policies.html

Question 13 | Bank ID: Y12-013 | Task: 1.2
You’re running a UDP- based application on an EC2 instance. How can you protect it against a DDoS attack?
A. Place the instance behind a network load balancer.
B. Implement a security group to restrict inbound access to the instance.
C. Place the instance behind an application load balancer.
D. Enable AWS Shield Standard.

Book answer: B
Practice answer: No unique defensible key assigned
Review: Review only | Scored practice: No
Rationale: Restricting allowed sources can reduce exposure, while DDoS protection depends on attack layer, traffic and protected resource types. Shield Standard is automatic rather than enabled as a separate step. A security group alone is not a complete volumetric DDoS solution.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Study note. An NLB is not, by itself, a complete protection policy for the application.
B: Study note. Restricting source access reduces exposure when legitimate clients are known.
C: Study note. ALB does not support arbitrary UDP applications.
D: Study note. Shield Standard is automatic; the stem's enable action is misleading and protections depend on resource type.

References:
AWS documentation: EC2 — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AWSEC2/latest/UserGuide/concepts.html

Question 14 | Bank ID: Y12-014 | Task: 1.2
You’re running a web application on six EC2 instances behind a network load balancer. The web application uses a MySQL database. How can you protect your application against SQL injection attacks? (Choose two.)
A. Enable WAF.
B. Assign elastic IP addresses to the instances.
C. Place the instances behind an application load balancer.
D. Block TCP port 3306.

Book answer: A, C
Practice answer: A, C
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: Move the HTTP application behind an ALB and associate AWS WAF with that supported resource to inspect SQL-injection patterns. WAF also supports other integrations; it cannot be directly attached to the existing NLB.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Correct. Attach a WAF web ACL to a supported resource such as an ALB. Move the HTTP application behind an ALB and associate AWS WAF with that supported resource to inspect SQL-injection patterns. WAF also supports other integrations; it cannot be directly attached to the existing NLB.
B: Incorrect for this question. Assigning EIPs does not inspect malicious SQL in HTTP requests.
C: Correct. Move the HTTP application behind an ALB and associate AWS WAF with that supported resource to inspect SQL-injection patterns. WAF also supports other integrations; it cannot be directly attached to the existing NLB.
D: Incorrect for this question. Blocking direct database traffic does not stop injection through permitted web requests.

Question 15 | Bank ID: Y12-015 | Task: 1.2
Which services protect against an HTTP flood attack?
A. GuardDuty
B. WAF
C. Shield Standard
D. Shield Advanced

Book answer: B, D
Practice answer: B, D
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: Both WAF and Shield Advanced can protect against HTTP flood attacks, which are marked by excessive or malformed requests. Shield Advanced includes WAF at no charge. Shield Standard does not offer protection against Layer 7 attacks. GuardDuty looks for signs of an attack but does not prevent one.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Incorrect for this question. GuardDuty detects threats rather than filters HTTP floods.
B: Correct. Both WAF and Shield Advanced can protect against HTTP flood attacks, which are marked by excessive or malformed requests. Shield Advanced includes WAF at no charge. Shield Standard does not offer protection against Layer 7 attacks. GuardDuty looks for signs of an attack but does not prevent one.
C: Incorrect for this question. Shield Standard addresses common network/transport attacks rather than configurable application-layer HTTP filtering.
D: Correct. Shield Advanced supports application-layer protections with applicable integrations. Both WAF and Shield Advanced can protect against HTTP flood attacks, which are marked by excessive or malformed requests. Shield Advanced includes WAF at no charge. Shield Standard does not offer protection against Layer 7 attacks. GuardDuty looks for signs of an attack but does not prevent one.

Question 16 | Bank ID: Y12-016 | Task: 1.3

C: Incorrect for this question. SSE-S3 is not the required customer-controlled KMS key.
D: Incorrect for this question. SSE-C keys are supplied directly to S3, not the requested KMS key model.

Question 18 | Bank ID: Y12-018 | Task: 1.3
You need to copy the data from an unencrypted EBS volume to another region and encrypt it. How can you accomplish this? (Choose two.)
A. Create an encrypted snapshot of the unencrypted volume.
B. Simultaneously encrypt and copy the snapshot to the destination region.
C. Copy the encrypted snapshot to the destination region.
D. Create an unencrypted snapshot of the unencrypted volume.

Book answer: B, D
Practice answer: B, D
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: To encrypt data on an unencrypted EBS volume, you must first take a snapshot. The snapshot will inherit the encryption characteristics of the source volume, so an unencrypted EBS volume will always yield an unencrypted snapshot. You can then simultaneously encrypt the snapshot as you copy it to another region.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Incorrect for this question. A snapshot of an unencrypted volume is unencrypted; encryption is set when copying.
B: Correct. To encrypt data on an unencrypted EBS volume, you must first take a snapshot. The snapshot will inherit the encryption characteristics of the source volume, so an unencrypted EBS volume will always yield an unencrypted snapshot. You can then simultaneously encrypt the snapshot as you copy it to another region.
C: Incorrect for this question. Copying a separately encrypted snapshot is possible but is a different workflow than the requested pair.
D: Correct. To encrypt data on an unencrypted EBS volume, you must first take a snapshot. The snapshot will inherit the encryption characteristics of the source volume, so an unencrypted EBS volume will always yield an unencrypted snapshot. You can then simultaneously encrypt the snapshot as you copy it to another region.

References:
AWS documentation: EBS — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/ebs/latest/userguide/what-is-ebs.html

CHAPTER 12: Secure Architectures
Question 19 | Bank ID: Y12-019 | Task: 1.3
An instance with an unencrypted EBS volume has an unencrypted EFS filesystem mounted on it. You need to encrypt the data on an existing EFS filesystem using a KMS key. How can you accomplish this?
A. Encrypt the EBS volume of the instance.
B. Create a new encrypted EFS filesystem and copy the data to it.
C. Enable encryption on the existing EFS filesystem.
D. Use a third-p arty encryption program to encrypt the data.

Book answer: B
Practice answer: B
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: EFS native encryption at rest is chosen when creating the filesystem. Create an encrypted filesystem with the KMS key and migrate data. Encrypting an unrelated EBS disk does not encrypt EFS; application-level KMS encryption is a different model.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Incorrect for this question. Encrypting EBS does not change separately mounted EFS encryption.
B: Correct. EFS native encryption at rest is chosen when creating the filesystem. Create an encrypted filesystem with the KMS key and migrate data. Encrypting an unrelated EBS disk does not encrypt EFS; application-level KMS encryption is a different model.
C: Incorrect for this question. EFS encryption at rest cannot be turned on for an existing filesystem.
D: Incorrect for this question. Application encryption is possible, but it is not native EFS KMS encryption at rest.

References:
AWS documentation: EBS — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/ebs/latest/userguide/what-is-ebs.html
AWS documentation: EFS — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/efs/latest/ug/whatisefs.html
AWS documentation: KMS — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/kms/latest/developerguide/overview.html

Book answer: A

Question 5 | Bank ID: Y13-005 | Task: 1.1
Your company wants to more closely coordinate the administration of its multiple AWS accounts, and AWS Organizations can help it do that. How does that change your security profile? (Choose three.)
A. An organization- level administration account breach is potentially more damaging.
B. User permissions can be controlled centrally at the organization level.
C. You should upgrade to use only specially hardened organization- level VPCs.
D. Standard security best practices such as MFA and strong passwords are even more essential.

Book answer: A, B, D
Practice answer: A, B, D
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: As efficient as Organizations can be, so does the threat they represent grow. There is no such thing as a specially hardened organization- level VPC. Security groups don’t require any special configuration.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Correct. As efficient as Organizations can be, so does the threat they represent grow. There is no such thing as a specially hardened organization- level VPC. Security groups don’t require any special configuration.
B: Correct. SCPs constrain maximum member-account permissions; they do not grant permissions themselves. As efficient as Organizations can be, so does the threat they represent grow. There is no such thing as a specially hardened organization- level VPC. Security groups don’t require any special configuration.
C: Incorrect for this question. There is no mandatory specially hardened organization-only VPC type.

E: Incorrect for this question. Joining Organizations does not require replacing every security group.

Question 6 | Bank ID: Y13-006 | Task: 4.2
Which of the following resource states are monitored by AWS Trusted Advisor? (Choose two.)

Book answer: B, C

Rationale: Trusted Advisor monitors your EC2 instances for lower than 10 percent CPU and network I/O below 5 MB on four or more days. Trusted Advisor doesn’t monitor Route 53 hosted zones or the status of S3 data transfers. Proper OS- level configuration of your EC2 instances is your responsibility.

Trusted Advisor doesn’t monitor Route 53 hosted zones or the status of S3 data transfers. Proper OS- level configuration of your EC2 instances is your responsibility.
D: Incorrect for this question. Trusted Advisor does not generally inspect every Linux guest's SSH configuration.
E: Incorrect for this question. Encrypted transport enforcement can be checked by security tooling, but is not the stated classic TA check here.

Question 10 | Bank ID: Y13-010 | Task: 4.2
Your online web store normally requires three EC2 instances to handle traffic but experiences a twofold increase in traffic for the two summer months. Which of the following approaches makes the most sense?
A. Run three on-d emand instances 12 months per year and schedule six reserve instances for the summer months.
B. Run three spot instances for the summer months and three reserve instances 12 months/year.
C. Run nine reserve instances for 12 months/year.
D. Run three reserve instances 12 months/year and purchase three scheduled reserve instances for the summer months.

Book answer: D
Practice answer: D
Review: Corrected | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: Match a suitable one- or three-year RI/Savings Plans commitment to the steady three-instance baseline, and use three extra On-Demand instances only during the seasonal peak. Practice stem clarified; original retained below. Practice option wording repaired; source options retained below.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Incorrect for this question. Two-month reserved commitments are not a standard RI option.
B: Incorrect for this question. Spot can interrupt transactions and is not the safest seasonal capacity default.
C: Incorrect for this question. Nine year-round reservations greatly exceed the six-instance peak.
D: Correct. Scheduled Reserved Instances are no longer available and do not fit this current strategy. Match a suitable one- or three-year RI/Savings Plans commitment to the steady three-instance baseline, and use three extra On-Demand instances only during the seasonal peak.

Practice wording:
An uninterrupted web store needs three instances year-round and six total during two summer months. Which option combines baseline commitments with seasonal flexibility?
A. Run three on-d emand instances 12 months per year and schedule six reserve instances for the summer months.
B. Run three spot instances for the summer months and three reserve instances 12 months/year.
C. Run nine reserve instances for 12 months/year.
D. Use three matching reserved instances for the year-round baseline and three additional On-Demand instances during the summer peak.

Question 13 | Bank ID: Y13-013 | Task: 4.2
Which of the following benefits of containers such as Docker can significantly reduce your AWS compute costs? (Choose two.)
A. Containers can launch quickly.
B. Containers can deliver increased server density.
C. Containers make it easy to reliably replicate server environments.
D. Containers can run using less memory than physical machines.

Book answer: B, D
Practice answer: No unique defensible key assigned
Review: Review only | Scored practice: No
Rationale: Density and reduced per-workload overhead can save costs; fast launch can also reduce idle capacity. The comparison to physical-machine memory is imprecise, and the two-choice limit excludes another plausible cost benefit.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Study note. Fast startup can also reduce overprovisioning, so this is not inherently unrelated to cost.
B: Study note. Density and reduced per-workload overhead can save costs; fast launch can also reduce idle capacity. The comparison to physical-machine memory is imprecise, and the two-choice limit excludes another plausible cost benefit.
C: Study note. Replication consistency mainly improves delivery reliability rather than directly guarantees lower compute cost.
D: Study note. Containers share a host kernel, reducing per-workload overhead relative to full virtual machines.

Question 18 | Bank ID: Y13-018 | Task: 4.1
You need to make sure your EBS volumes are regularly backed up, but you’re afraid you’ll forget to remove older snapshot versions, leading to expensive data bloat. What’s the best solution to this problem?
A. Configure the EBS Lifecycle Manager.
B. Create a script that will regularly invoke the AWS CLI to prune older snapshots.

There is no “EBS Scheduled Reserve In- stance” but there is an “EC2 Scheduled Reserve Instance.” Tying a string? Really? EBS snap- shots are stored in S3, but you can’t access the buckets that they’re kept in.

Creating a script is possible, but it’s nowhere near as simple, and it’s not tightly integrated with your AWS infrastructure. There is no “EBS Scheduled Reserve In- stance” but there is an “EC2 Scheduled Reserve Instance.” Tying a string? Really? EBS snap- shots are stored in S3, but you can’t access the buckets that they’re kept in.
B: Incorrect for this question. Custom scripts add maintenance compared with managed snapshot retention policies.
C: Incorrect for this question. Scheduled Reserved Instances are unrelated to EBS snapshots.
D: Incorrect for this question. A reminder does not automate reliable backups and retention.
E: Incorrect for this question. S3 lifecycle rules cannot manage EBS snapshot resources.

Question 19 | Bank ID: Y13-019 | Task: 4.2
Which of these AWS CLI commands will launch a spot fleet?
A. aws ec2 request- fleet - - spot- fleet- request- config file:// Config.json
B. aws ec2 spot- fleet - - spot- fleet- request- config file:// Config.json
C. aws ec2 launch- spot- fleet - - spot- fleet- request- config/ file:// Config.json
D. aws ec2 request- spot- fleet - - spot- fleet- request- config/ file:// Config.json

Book answer: D
Practice answer: D
Review: Corrected | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: request-spot-fleet invokes the Spot Fleet API with a JSON configuration document. The other listed subcommands are not the correct API command. Practice option wording repaired; source options retained below.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Incorrect for this question. request-fleet is not the Spot Fleet API subcommand.
B: Incorrect for this question. spot-fleet is not the request subcommand.
C: Incorrect for this question. launch-spot-fleet is not the request subcommand.
D: Correct. The intended command is aws ec2 request-spot-fleet --spot-fleet-request-config file://Config.json. request-spot-fleet invokes the Spot Fleet API with a JSON configuration document. The other listed subcommands are not the correct API command.

Practice wording:
Which of these AWS CLI commands will launch a spot fleet?
A. aws ec2 request- fleet - - spot- fleet- request- config file:// Config.json
B. aws ec2 spot- fleet - - spot- fleet- request- config file:// Config.json
C. aws ec2 launch- spot- fleet - - spot- fleet- request- config/ file:// Config.json
D. aws ec2 request-spot-fleet --spot-fleet-request-config file://Config.json

References:
AWS reference for this review — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/cli/latest/reference/ec2/request-spot-fleet.html
AWS documentation: EC2 — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AWSEC2/latest/UserGuide/concepts.html

CHAPTER 13: Cost-Optimized Architectures
Question 20 | Bank ID: Y13-020 | Task: 4.2
Which of the following elements is not something you’d include in your spot fleet request?
A. Availability zone
B. Target capacity
C. Platform (the instance OS)
D. AMI

Book answer: C
Practice answer: C
Review: Reviewed | Scored practice: Yes
Rationale: The availability zone, target capacity, and AMI are all elements of a complete spot fleet request.

Option-by-option reasons:
A: Incorrect for this question. Availability Zone or subnet placement can be specified.
B: Incorrect for this question. The availability zone, target capacity, and AMI are all elements of a complete spot fleet request.
C: Correct. The AMI implies operating-system platform; it is not a standalone mandatory request field. The availability zone, target capacity, and AMI are all elements of a complete spot fleet request.
D: Incorrect for this question. An AMI identifies the image to launch.

References:
AWS documentation: EC2 — https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AWSEC2/latest/UserGuide/concepts.html
